2026-09-27 22:22:33 · Autor
What does 12,638 are gate-only mean
2026-09-27 22:22:38 · Werkzeug Bash:
2026-09-27 22:22:49 · Claude
Those 12,638 terms are in the file only because of the **gate**. None of them is a mapping.

Each term record in the mapping file can answer two separate questions:

1. **Where does this term belong?** This is the mapping: the `to` target plus the `pairs` verdicts from pass 1, pass 2 and you.
2. **Does this term belong in the ontology at all?** This is the `gate` section: `in` or `out`.

A "gate-only" record answers only question 2. It has no `to` and no `pairs`, because nothing ever proposed merging the term with another. The record exists just to hold its in/out verdict. For example:

```
"10 m walk test": {
  "gate": {"pass2": {"verdict": "out", "reason": "Measurement instrument/assessment method, not wound-specific."},
           "rule":  {"verdict": "out", "by": "METHODOLOGY_BLOCKLIST", "when": "imported 2026-09-25 … provenance unknown"}}}
```

Where the verdicts on those 12,638 come from:

| Verdict source | Records | in / out |
|---|---|---|
| `rule` only: the old `METHODOLOGY_BLOCKLIST` and `WHITELIST`, folded in on 25 Sep with no provenance | 9,252 | 2,418 in / 7,159 out* |
| gate pass 2 only: the model's scope verdict of 25 Sep | 3,054 | 570 in / 2,808 out* |
| both `rule` and gate pass 2 | 324 | |
| you | 8 | 5 in / 3 out |

\*The in/out counts include the 324 records that have both sources.

What this means for the passes:
- **Mapping pass 1 and pass 2 have nothing to do on these records.** There's no pair to judge, so they aren't a backlog for steps 1–2.
- **They're gate records, so the gate passes apply to them.** 9,252 have only a list verdict with no known author, and no model or person has ever looked at them. That's the same problem rule 40 describes: `silver sulfadiazine` and `tissue repair` were on the blocklist.
- The gate backlog is also much bigger than these records. Of the 85,492 names in the store, 78,944 have no gate record at all, which is why the gate steps (3–4) are the large job.

Mapping pass 2 (step 2) is still running: 200 of 853 were asked at the last update. The client is retrying the timeouts. I'll report when it finishes, and then I need your call on the collagen reversal.
2026-09-27 22:24:21 · Autor
What about the thousands of extracted concepts in the concept store? Are they all mapped?
2026-09-27 22:24:26 · Werkzeug Bash:
2026-09-27 22:24:29 · Werkzeug Bash:
2026-09-27 22:24:38 · Werkzeug Bash:
2026-09-27 22:24:57 · Claude
No, and most of them shouldn't be. A mapping is a claim that two names are synonyms, so a concept with no synonym correctly has no mapping entry. The real questions are whether each name was checked for synonyms and whether the mappings have reached the store. Against the current store (`concept_store_2026-09-27_15-57.json`: 85,492 names from 6,029 abstracts):

| Status | Names | What it means |
|---|---|---|
| Checked by SapBERT, no synonym found (cosine ≥ 0.95) | 74,002 | Each stands as its own concept. 64,854 of them occur in only one abstract. |
| Mapped onto another name (live key) | 3,729 | Will be merged into its canonical. |
| Canonical that others map onto | 3,149 | |
| Mapping retired or refused | 1,972 | The name keeps its own identity. |
| **Never checked by SapBERT** | **2,640** | Not in the vector store: the name arrived or was renamed after the last build (26 Sep 17:46). It has had no chance to be mapped. |

(Some names in the mapped, canonical and retired rows are also missing from the vector store, 463 in all. Those mappings came from string normalisation or earlier builds, not from the last embedding run.)

Every name has been through pass 0; the dry run confirmed the fixed point.

What still needs doing:

1. **The mappings haven't been applied to the store.** 3,729 of the 3,849 live mapping keys are still names in the store, so the store holds the old spelling beside the canonical. Nothing downstream sees these merges until `run_apply_mapping` runs. That should come after passes 1–2 and the curator review are settled, not before.
2. **2,640 names need a mapping build.** It's incremental, since the other 88k vectors are cached. Its new pairs then go through pass 1 and pass 2 like the rest.
3. **Recall is an open question.** The build only proposes pairs at cosine ≥ 0.95, and nothing measures the synonyms below that line. The 74,002 unmapped names are "no neighbour above 0.95", not "has no synonym". If you want a number, a sample of them could be checked for synonyms the threshold missed.

Mapping pass 2 is still running in the background. I'll report when it's done, and the collagen reversal still needs your call.

**Collagen: which name should the concept have?** Three terms are involved:
- `Collagen-based dressing → collagen dressing` came from SapBERT, pass 1 said `same_class`, and you haven't ruled on it.
- `collagen matrix dressing → collagen dressing` is there because today's queue had you map `collagen matrix dressing → Collagen-based dressing`, and chain resolution followed that on to `collagen dressing`.
- Today you also **refused** `Collagen-based dressing → collagen matrix dressing`.

Reversing literally (`collagen dressing → collagen matrix dressing`) leaves `Collagen-based dressing → collagen dressing → collagen matrix dressing`. Resolving that chain produces the exact pair you refused. So:
- **A.** Name it `collagen matrix dressing`, with all three terms mapped onto it. This lifts today's refusal.
- **B.** Name it `Collagen-based dressing`, with the other two mapped onto it. This matches today's queue decision.
- **C.** Make two concepts. `collagen matrix dressing` stands alone, which is pass 1's "narrower" reading, and the other two stay merged.

Whichever you pick, `collagen dressing` is on the old blocklist as `out`, with no known author. If it ends up as the name or a member, that gate verdict probably needs your ruling too.

It couldn't say which spelling a given abstract used once several merged, it never kept the extractor's literal string, it didn't record renames of relation targets, and a mapping rename would have been stamped only `by: "mapping"`. Now:

- **The existing 6,029 abstracts were backfilled.** Their literal strings were never stored, so the book starts from the earliest surviving snapshot (22 Sep), and each abstract is stamped with that source. Every one of the 214,813 names found its origin. The store was backed up first, and I confirmed the result on the saved file.
- **Tests:** 9 new tests, and each of 8 deliberate breakages of the new code is caught. The unit suite passes: 1,683 tests plus the whitelist check after the fix. I added the design as rule 50 in `INSTRUCTIONS.md`.

The first example it surfaced is a problem: abstract 42553988 wrote `Compound Muscle Action Potentials`, and the store now reads `Compound Muscle Activity Potential`. That's rule 49's `action → activity` vocabulary rewriting a fixed technical term. Other compounds like `action potential` are probably affected too.

1. **Decide on `action → activity`.** Either exempt `action potential`-style terms or drop that rule, before the store gets more renames.
2. **Apply the mapping to the store.** Every live pair is now judged or ruled on, and each of the 3,729 renames will carry its evidence and origins.

4. **Gate pass 2 on the names with no scope verdict,** starting with the 1,287 that appear in ≥10 abstracts.
5. **An incremental mapping build for the 2,640 names never checked,** then pass 1 and pass 2 on the new pairs.

2026-09-28 01:34:07 · Claude
While the rehearsal runs, here is the concept store's architecture as it stands now.

**Concept store: `data/cache/concept_store_<timestamp>.json`, 6,029 abstracts**

```
{
  "<PMID>": {                                   ← one record per abstract (6,029)
    "entities": {                               ← what the abstract asserts
      "<name>": { "<predicate>: <target>": 1.0, … }
    },
    "as_extracted": {                           ← NEW (rule 50): what it actually said
      "<name as spelled now>": ["<string the extractor wrote>", …]
    },
    "as_extracted_from": "snapshot 2026-09-22 …",   ← only on the backfilled 6,029
    "meta_stamp": "…",                          ← extraction run / model stamp
    "schema_report": {                          ← 2,840 abstracts: what the schema refused
      "rejected_terms", "rejected_relations", "unknown_predicates" }
  },
  "decisions": {                                ← the store's own ledger (never per abstract)
    "entity_verdicts": { "<name>": {status, by, note, when} },          2,330 (all blocklist)
    "cell_verdicts":   { "<name>": { "<triple>": {status, by, …} } },   6,436 (all blocklist)
    "renamed":         { "<old>": {to, by, when, evidence?, history?} } 38,289
  }
}
```

It works in three layers:

1. **Assertions** (`entities`): 176,808 entity records carrying 265,687 relation cells, all positive (`1.0`). Nothing an abstract said is ever deleted (rule 33).
2. **Verdicts** (`decisions.entity_verdicts` and `cell_verdicts`): a rejection sits beside the assertion it rejects, with who, why and when. Readers go through `records()` and `assertions()`, which apply the verdicts. So the raw file keeps everything, and every consumer sees the store minus whatever was rejected. Today every verdict comes from the blocklist.
3. **Names and their history:**
   - `as_extracted` is **per abstract**. It says what this abstract wrote.
   - `renamed` is **global**. It says why a spelling moved: pass 0, the curator, and soon the mapping with its evidence.

   Together they trace any name from the abstract's string to the current label and the decision behind each step.

Synthetic records (`predefined`, `llm_fill`, `llm_fill_nan`, `fca_promoted`) can sit beside the PMIDs. None exists in the store right now.

**What the store feeds:** every downstream artefact is a view rebuilt from it: the context matrix, the FCA lattice, the class and part-of hierarchies, and the OWL export. The **term mapping and gate** file is its companion. It holds where each term belongs and whether it belongs at all. Applying the mapping moves names in the store and stamps each move in both books.

**Status of your two requests:**
1. **`action potential` is exempt from `action → activity`.** A protected phrase guards it, with 5 new tests, mutation-checked. The six names the rule had already rewritten are renamed back in the store, the books and the mapping, e.g. `Compound Muscle Activity Potential → Compound Muscle Action Potential`. Their origins survived, and pass 0 is still at a fixed point.
2. **Mapping apply: rehearsal in progress.** I'm running it on an in-memory copy before touching the real store, because `normalize` also title-cases names. I want to know how many names would change casing alone, undoing pass 0's chosen spelling, before it writes anything. The copy is running slowly because your three disparities MCMC jobs are using most of the CPU. I'll report the numbers and then apply.
2026-09-28 01:43:42 · Autor
What do you mean with renames is global? So something sits next to the Pmid keys which is not a pmid key?
2026-09-28 01:43:55 · Claude
Yes. Besides the 6,029 PMID keys there is exactly one key that isn't a PMID: `"decisions"`. It's reserved: the code names it with the constant `ConceptStore.DECISIONS`, and `document_keys()` returns every key except it. So the top level looks like this:

```
{
  "42553988": { entities, as_extracted, meta_stamp, … },   ← an abstract
  "42553990": { … },                                       ← an abstract
  …                                                        (6,029 of them)
  "decisions": {                                           ← the one non-PMID key
      "entity_verdicts": …,
      "cell_verdicts":   …,
      "renamed":         …
  }
}
```

By "global" I mean `renamed` holds **one entry per spelling for the whole store**, not one per abstract. It's keyed by the old spelling:

```
"renamed": {
  "Cuts":                               {"to": "cut", "by": "pass0", "when": "2026-09-26T15:13:15"},
  "Compound Muscle Activity Potential": {"to": "Compound Muscle Action Potential",
                                         "by": "curator 2026-09-27: action potential exempt …", …}
}
```

`Cuts → cut` is recorded once and applies to every abstract that contained `Cuts`. The book says nothing about which abstracts those were. That's the per-abstract `as_extracted` book's job, inside each PMID record.

The two verdict books work the same way. `entity_verdicts["\"compartment syndrome\""]` rejects that name wherever it occurs, and `cell_verdicts` rejects one relation of one name everywhere. They sit beside the PMIDs because a verdict is about a name, not about one abstract.

Why it's in the same file rather than a separate one: rule 33 says the store is the one record. A rejection or a rename kept in a side file can drift from the data it describes, or get left behind when the store is copied or rolled back. The cost is that anything iterating over the raw dict must skip `"decisions"`. The store's own readers (`document_keys()`, `records()`, `assertions()`) do. Scripts that loop over `store.dict` directly have to check `key == store.DECISIONS`, and the ones I've touched today do.

If you'd rather have per-abstract rename history instead of the global book, each PMID record could get its own `renamed` list. But `as_extracted` already answers the per-abstract question ("what did this abstract write, and what does it read as now"), and the global book avoids repeating `Cuts → cut` in every abstract that mentions cuts.

The mapping rehearsal is still running. I'll report its numbers before anything is written to the store.

2026-09-28 01:48:05 · Claude
Before proposing anything, I'm checking whether today's store could be rebuilt exactly from the untouched 22 Sep snapshot. That decides whether the cleaner design is reachable without losing anything:
2026-09-28 01:48:10 · Werkzeug Bash:
2026-09-28 01:48:51 · Claude
**My recommendation: stop rewriting the abstracts. Keep them exactly as extracted, put every decision about a term in the term file, and compute the normalised store from the two.** You're right that the overlap is odd. Here is the proposal.

**Why it feels wrong.** Two files decide things about the same terms:

| Question | Concept store | Term mapping and gate |
|---|---|---|
| What is this string called? | `decisions.renamed` (38k pass 0 renames) | `to` (3,849 mappings) |
| Is it out of scope? | `decisions.entity_verdicts` (2,330 blocklist rejections) | `gate` (`rule: out`, the same blocklist) |
| What did the abstract say? | `as_extracted`, carried through every rename | — |

The root cause is that the store is **rewritten in place**. Pass 0, the mapping apply and the sense split each overwrite the abstracts' names. Once the evidence is overwritten, you need a provenance book to remember what it was, and a rename log to explain why it moved. `as_extracted` and `renamed` exist only to compensate for that.

The rest of the pipeline already works the other way. The hierarchies, the lattice and the OWL export are views rebuilt from a record (rule 38). The store is the one place that breaks the pattern.

**Proposal: two records and one view.**

```
EVIDENCE  (concept store, keyed by PMID only)        LEDGER  (term mapping & gate, keyed by term)
  what each abstract said, never edited                every decision about a term
  {pmid: {entities: {raw: {pred: raw_target}},         {raw or canonical term:
          meta_stamp, schema_report}}                     spelling:  {to, by: pass0, when}     ← was store.renamed
                                                          to/pairs:  mapping + pass/curator    ← as now
                        \                                 gate:      in/out, all sources        ← absorbs entity_verdicts
                         \                                relations: {"pred: target": verdict}  ← was cell_verdicts
                          \                               senses:    per-category split names } ← was the in-place split
                           \                          /
                            → VIEW = evidence resolved through the ledger
                              raw → spelling → mapping → gate/relation verdicts
                              rebuilt on demand, never edited, never saved as the truth
```

Your three questions:
- **Keep the PMID keys?** Yes, and only PMID keys. The abstract is the unit of evidence. `"decisions"` leaves the file, so nothing non-PMID sits beside them.
- **A third dict?** No. Every decision is about a term, and the term file already holds one record per term. Adding a third file would recreate the overlap.
- **Provenance in the mapping?** Provenance stops being a book you maintain and becomes a lookup. The evidence still holds `Compound Muscle Action Potentials` in PMID 42553988. The ledger says it's spelled `…Action Potential` (pass 0) and which canonical it maps to (entry, method, verdicts). Asking "where does this concept come from" means reading the ledger backwards to the raw strings and then to the PMIDs.

**Evidence that it works:** I rebuilt today's store from the 22 Sep snapshot using only the recorded name decisions. All **265,687** current relation cells were reproduced, with nothing missing and nothing extra. So the snapshot plus a ledger is today's store, exactly, and moving to this design loses no data.

**Costs:**
- **It revisits one of your decisions.** On 26 Sep you asked pass 0 to change labels in place rather than create mapping entries. The reason was to keep mechanical respellings from burying the real mapping decisions. The ledger keeps that separation: respellings go in `spelling`, and the review sheets read only `to`/`pairs`. But labels would change in the view, not in the evidence.
- **Everything reads the view.** Readers already go through `records()` and `assertions()`. The work is making those resolve through the ledger, plus finding scripts that read `store.dict` directly.
- **The sense split becomes a ledger rule.** It depends on how each abstract typed a term, so it's computed when the view is built, not stored as renamed names.
- **I'd remove `as_extracted`,** the book I built today. The untouched evidence makes it redundant.
- **Rough effort:** one to two days of work, including a check that the new view reproduces today's store exactly, as it did above.

**What I'm holding in the meantime:** the mapping apply. It's another in-place rewrite of the abstracts, which this design replaces. If you go with the proposal, the mapping takes effect through the view, and nothing has to be applied to the store at all. The rehearsal is still running, and its numbers are still useful as a check on the new view.

Should I go ahead with this design? I can also write it up as a page if you want it for your notes or your supervisor.
2026-09-28 02:08:27 · Autor
I know concept store and termmapping and gate dict. Where is the ledger?
2026-09-28 02:08:35 · Claude
The ledger is **your existing term mapping and gate dict**. I shouldn't have given it a new name: there's no third file. The proposal only moves things between the two files you already have.

**Concept store (`concept_store_*.json`)** becomes pure evidence:
- keeps: the PMID keys, `entities` as the extractor wrote them, `meta_stamp`, `schema_report`
- loses: the `"decisions"` key (its three books move to the term dict), the in-place renaming, and `as_extracted`

**Term mapping and gate dict (`term_mapping_*.json`)** keeps what it holds now and gains three sections per term record:

```
"Compound Muscle Action Potentials": {
  "spelling":  {"to": "Compound Muscle Action Potential", "by": "pass0", "when": …},   ← from store decisions.renamed
  "to": …, "pairs": {…},                                                              ← mapping, as now
  "gate": {"rule": …, "pass2": …, "curator": …},                                      ← as now; absorbs store entity_verdicts
  "relations": {"is_a: Neoplasm": {"status": "rejected", "by": "blocklist", …}}       ← from store decisions.cell_verdicts
}
```

The sense split would also become a field on the term's record, instead of names rewritten inside the abstracts.

With that, the concept store answers only "what did each abstract say", and the term dict answers everything we decided about a term. The normalised store you work with is computed from the two whenever it's needed.

Done: the concept store now holds only what the abstracts said, and every decision lives in the term mapping and gate dict. The mapping is applied through the view, as you asked in point 2. Nothing had to be rewritten to apply it: the 83,162 names as spelled now read as 79,755. The `action potential` exemption from point 1 is also in, and the six names already rewritten are back.

**The two files now**

| File | Holds |
|---|---|
| **Concept store** | Only the 6,029 PMID records, with entities and relations as the abstracts wrote them. It's never rewritten. |
| **Term mapping and gate dict** | Every decision, on the term's record. New sections: `spelling` (an old spelling now written as X, with who and when), `rejected`, `relations`, `senses`. |

Everything downstream reads the view: the abstracts' strings resolved through the term dict (spelling, then mapping, then rejections). Provenance is now a lookup: `store.trace("Collagen-based dressing")` lists every string the abstracts wrote for it, their PMIDs, each spelling step with its author, and your ruling behind the merge.

**Is it safe?** Nothing was written until the new view reproduced the old store exactly: 6,029 abstracts and 265,687 cells, with and without rejections. I checked again after reading the files back from disk. Both old files are backed up in `data/backup/`. Pass 0 still finds nothing to rename, and the unit suite passes: 1,691 tests plus 33 rerun after the migration.

**What moved during the migration**
- **Rename log:** the store's old rename log became 38,262 alias records.
- **Reconstructed:** 695 more renames happened before relation-target renames were recorded. They're rebuilt and marked "reconstructed".
- **Reversed:** 27 renames were later undone (e.g. `histopathological/histopathologic`, `action potential`). They're kept as history, not aliases.
- **Rejections:** 2,330 rejected terms and 6,436 rejected relations moved onto their terms' records.
- **Evidence source:** each abstract comes from the 22 Sep snapshot, the earliest version kept. The extractor's exact output was never saved, and each abstract is stamped with that.

**Decisions I made that you should know about**
- **No more automatic title-casing.** Names keep the spelling pass 0 chose. As a result, mapping entries that differ only by case or plural now change names and are no longer "inert".
- **New extractions store the extractor's exact string.** Ingestion's clean-up is recorded as a spelling decision. It never touches a name the store already has, or an ontology label like `vitamins`.
- **Rejections are not merged into the gate.** Your 2,330 store rejections and the gate's 10,531 `out` verdicts answer the same question but stay separate. Merging them would drop about 8,000 more names from everything downstream at once, so that's your call.

**Also changed**
- `as_extracted` from earlier today is gone; the untouched abstracts make it redundant.
- Two scripts are retired to `data/removed_code/`.
- The design is rule 51 in `INSTRUCTIONS.md`.
- I updated my memory note on the store design.

2026-09-28 12:13:58 · Claude
No, restarting for speed isn't worth it, and the cap turned out to be credit, not requests per second. Your OpenRouter account ran out of credit mid-run, so I stopped the whole chain.

**The cap.** OpenRouter shows no request-rate limit on your key. It limits how much credit your requests can hold at once. Each request reserves credit for the longest answer it might produce, and our code sets no maximum, so every call reserves room for up to 131,072 tokens. All reservations in flight have to fit in your remaining balance. More parallel requests means more reservations, so raising the parallelism would only have produced more failures.

**Why it stopped.** The account has $60.00 of credit and $60.17 used. The daily key limit isn't the issue ($0.39 used of $50). Every request was failing with "402: exceeds available credits", 14 batches had already failed, and the mapping build would have hit the same wall next.

**Saved on disk:** 7,284 pass 2 verdicts from today: the 1,084 from the unaudited queue plus about 6,200 undecided terms, up to the checkpoint at batch 250. The roughly 20 batches after that checkpoint were lost, about 500 terms or a few cents. They'll simply be asked again.

**Cost to finish:** today's 290 batches cost $0.39, so the remaining ~2,800 batches come to about $4. The mapping build's adjudication and the second opinion add a few dollars more. About $10 of credit at openrouter.ai/settings/credits covers all of it.

Once you've topped up:
1. I restart from the undecided queue. It skips everything already answered.
2. Then the mapping build, the second opinion on its new pairs, and the blocklist/whitelist re-export.

One improvement worth making first: cap each request's maximum answer length, so each request reserves only a small amount of credit. That makes a low balance far less likely to reject requests. I'd first measure how long the model's answers really are, since cutting an answer short would break its JSON.

Your [Scope Rulings](https://claude.ai/artifact/38t9J9DRGJ3HCsTtQ5V2kz) page is unaffected; you can keep ruling on the 722 there.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Rule 51 architecture**:
     - Concept store = evidence per PMID, never rewritten.
     - TermMappingAndGate records carry `spelling` (alias), `to`/`pairs`, `gate`, `rejected` (non-scope only), `relations`, `senses`, `spelling_reversed`.
     - `ConceptStore.view(mapped, include_rejected)` resolves: raw → spelling aliases → live mapping (exact, then folded key) → verdicts → senses.
     - Scope is read live via `store.scope` (the cleaner's `_is_blocked` Blocker).
   - **Passes**:
     - Pass 0: deterministic spelling renames, now written as aliases.
     - Mapping pass 1/pass 2: LLM adjudication / second opinion.
     - Gate pass 1: SapBERT nearest decided term, floor 0.95.
     - Gate pass 2: LLM scope verdict, `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash` via OpenRouter.
   - Curator precedence: curator > passes > rule.
   - Artifacts with the db capability; the applier reads decisions via ArtifactData.
   - **OpenRouter in-flight credit reservation**: requests without max_tokens reserve 131,072 tokens each; this caused 402s when the balance was low.
   - Standing rules: no silent failure; verify by reading back from disk; mutation-check tests; append a rule to INSTRUCTIONS.md for each universal design choice; back up to `data/backup/` before writes.

3. Files and Code Sections (repo root `/Users/Strubbelig/Library/Mobile Documents/com~apple~CloudDocs/Forschung/Ontology/Wound ontology`):

   - **src/wound_ontology/store/concept_store.py** (major rewrite, rule 51)
     - `store_name(term, canonical)`: returns the declared category if the term is one; else the canonical (declared) or the term. No title-casing.
     - `_NoFile`, `_is_typing`, `_sense_of`, `_sense_key` (smart_title), `_apply_senses`.
     - `records_of(store)` uses `store.records()` when available.
     - `__init__(dm, file_name, terms=None)`:
       - raises ValueError if the file still has a `decisions` record ("run scripts/p4_split_evidence_and_decisions.py").
       - sets `_terms`, `_live_mapping = terms is not None`, `_mapper = None`, `self.scope = None`.
     - Lazy `terms` property creates an in-memory TermMappingAndGate.
     - `_mapping(mapped)`, `resolver(mapped)` returning (spelled, final).
     - `_scope_test()`:
       - uses `getattr(self, "scope", None)`;
       - defaults to `terms.effective_gate_verdict(n)[0]=="out"`;
       - memoised.
     - `view()`: excludes an entity if a `rejected` verdict applies or `out_of_scope(owners)`; excludes a cell if its target is out of scope or it has a `relations` verdict; merges with max.
     - `records()` = `view()`; `effective_dict`, `assertions`, `trace(name)`.
     - `decisions()` is a read-only synthesized view.
     - `rejected_entities`, `rejected_cells`, `verdict`, `reject_entities`, `reject_cells`, `lift_decisions`, `restore_decisions` all read/write term dict records and call `terms.save()`.
     - `record_rename` → `terms.spell`.
     - `summary`: renamed = alias count.
     - `entities_raw` reads the view with `include_rejected`.
     - `normalize(mapper)` attaches the mapper (no rewrite; warns on folded collisions).
     - `apply_renames` delegates to `terms.apply_renames` and adds renamed/entities/merged verdicts to the report.
     - Ingestion stores literal strings, and `_spell_ingested(payload, settled)` records `by="ingestion"` aliases. It skips settled names and strings carrying decisions. `settled` is computed in `add_llm_extracts` from the view.
     - `split_irreconcilable_terms` writes `senses` to term records, keyed by smart_title.
     - Removed: `as_extracted`, `note_extracted`, `carry_extracted`, `_rewrite_names`.

   - **src/wound_ontology/mappers/term_mapping_and_gate.py**
     - `SPELLING = "spelling"`; `DECISION_SECTIONS = ("to","pairs","gate","gate_contested","rejected","relations","senses","retired")`.
     - `_load` keeps alias records as-is.
     - `record()` resolves aliases.
     - New: `is_alias`, `aliases`, `resolve` (raises on a cycle), `spell(old,new,*,by,evidence,when)`. `spell` refuses if old holds decisions or if it would close a cycle, and keeps history.
     - `apply_renames`:
       - leaves alias records for every renamed string, including strings with no record;
       - updates existing aliases' `to`;
       - renames `relations` triple targets;
       - raises on an alias/merged clash;
       - doesn't delete records holding gate/rejected/relations/senses;
       - report key `aliases`.
     - `_absorb` merges rejected/relations/senses.

Lernergebnisse, Qualifikations- und Kompetenzziele
Die Absolventen besitzen die Fähigkeit zur Bearbeitung komplexer Fragestellungen, Umsetzung wissenschaftlicher Erkenntnisse und Durchführung strategischer Führungsaufgaben. Die Masterarbeit soll zeigen, dass die Studierenden in der Lage sind, innerhalb einer vorgegebenen Frist eine fachliche Fragestellung sowohl in den fachlichen Einzelheiten als auch in den fachübergreifenden Zusammenhängen eigenständig mit wissenschaftlichen Methoden zu bearbeiten. Die Aufgabenstellung sollte aus den Bereichen Biomedizinische Informatik oder Medical Data Science stammen.

2026-09-28 16:43:00 · Claude
Written for: your examiners and supervisor, as a scientific thesis.

I've drafted the whole thesis as a first version of about 20,000 words, roughly 55–60 pages once the figures and tables are in. The chapters are in [Thesis/chapters/](Thesis/chapters/) and [Thesis/README.md](Thesis/README.md) lists what is in each file. Results that depend on the final rebuild are marked «interim» or left as `[…]`.

**What's there**
- **Structure.** It follows your story: flap ontology → a wound needs a disease model → evaluation of existing disease models → MILD → the curated wound ontology (WONT) → the literature pipeline. Then results, discussion and conclusion. There is an English abstract and a German Zusammenfassung.
- **Introduction.** [01_introduction.md](Thesis/chapters/01_introduction.md) follows the argument of *Modelling_Considerations.docx* step by step, with the flap ontology and the wound question around it.
- **MIRO.** MILD and WONT are reported in MIRO sections, with item-by-item checklists in Appendix A/B. I took each item's must/should level from the MIRO paper itself. The competency-question appendix is generated from the JSON file.
- **Engineering.** [06_ontology_learning.md](Thesis/chapters/06_ontology_learning.md) explains the design principles, the corpus and extraction, and the evidence/decision data architecture and why it replaced rewriting in place. It then covers normalisation, synonym adjudication, curator review, scope classification, reasoner checks and FCA. It ends with a table of silent defects found during development.
- **Terminology.** The text doesn't use the project glossary words. I checked for them automatically; a few "retired" remain in Appendix C, where the competency-question file is quoted as written. It uses standard terms instead: evidence store, terminology decision register, resolved evidence (a view), deterministic normalisation, first and second adjudication, scope classification, preferred term.
- **Numbers.** Every figure comes from a new read-only script, [scripts/thesis_figures.py](Wound%20ontology/scripts/thesis_figures.py). Its dated output goes to `output/`. Re-run it after the final rebuild to replace the interim numbers.

**Diagrams (PlantUML)**
- Your existing class and package diagrams dated from May and still showed the Gemini client. I regenerated them from the current code; the old ones are backed up in `data/backup/uml_2026-05-02/`.
- I added two new diagrams:
  - [dictionaries_wound_ontology.plantuml](Wound%20ontology/docs/uml/dictionaries_wound_ontology.plantuml): how both dictionaries are structured, and how an assertion is resolved when read.
  - [architecture_wound_ontology.plantuml](Wound%20ontology/docs/uml/architecture_wound_ontology.plantuml): the components and how data flows between them.
- Both are rendered to SVG and PNG in `Thesis/figures/`.

**Decisions you need to make or check**
1. **Language.** The module description says Deutsch. Please confirm that English is allowed.
2. **Study 1 is called a cross-sectional evaluation, not a scoping review.** That matches your own reporting-guideline note and the JAMIA draft.
3. **Reviewers will notice that extraction quality was never measured.** No precision or recall against hand-annotated abstracts exists. I recommend annotating about 50 abstracts before submission, and the limitations section says this.
4. **MIRO "must" items that are still open:**
   - a public repository and issue tracker;
   - versioning, deprecation and sustainability policies;
   - textual definitions, which only 140 of 441 WONT classes and 31 of 57 MILD classes have;
   - the DL expressivity statement;
   - the final HermiT result on the extended ontology.
5. **Citations marked `[verify]`** aren't in your library yet. Supervisor name and journal status for the flap paper are placeholders.

**Pipeline status**
- The scope classification of the undecided terms is at 1,920 of 2,820 batches, with 46,544 terms answered, 1,456 left unanswered and none failed.
- It has slowed to about 5 batches a minute, so it should finish around 16:00. The mapping build, second opinion and list export then start automatically.
- Credit estimate: about $2–3 should remain for those steps. It's still tight, as I said earlier.

2026-09-28 17:10:13 · Claude
You're asked because the page lists every term where the model's scope answer contradicts the old exclusion list. It doesn't matter which answer is currently applied.

**Why both rows are on the page**
- `biologic therapy` and `blood vessel formation` are both on the old methodology exclusion list, where nobody recorded who added them. The model now says "in" for both.
- The model outranks that old list, so "in" already applies. The page asks you to confirm the reversal. If you skip the row, "in" stands.
- This covers almost the whole page: in 720 of the 722 rows, the only dissent is the old list. Only 2 rows are real disagreements between model answers.
- I can rebuild the page with just those 2 rows, or those plus the old-list reversals sorted by how often they occur. Existing answers stay attached because row ids don't change.

**The naming problem, which the scope page can't fix**
The page only decides in or out. It can't rename or merge terms. Neither of your cases would have been merged automatically:
- **biologic → biological.**
  - The automatic spelling rules only handle plural, British/American spelling, case and hyphens. "-ic" versus "-ical" is a different word form, not one of those.
  - The meaning comparison nearly catches it, but not quite: `biologic therapy` against `biological therapy` scores 0.949, just under the 0.95 cut-off.
  - Worse, the earlier mapping went the other way: `Biological` is currently mapped onto `biologic`.
  - The fix is a fixed preferred-word rule, like your existing action → activity rule.
  - This one runs against your usual preference: you chose dermatologic, oncologic, pharmacologic, physiologic and radiologic over their "-ical" forms.
  - Your direction is still the safer one. It changes 48 names; the reverse would change 158, including "biological process", GO's name for that class.
  - I'd protect the product name `XCM BIOLOGIC Tissue Matrix` from the rule.
- **blood vessel formation → Angiogenesis process.**
  - The data says `blood vessel formation`; the page wraps "formation" onto a second line, so only "form" was visible.
  - The model recognised it as angiogenesis, but it has no way to link the term to the class. It's a paraphrase, and the meaning comparison scores only 0.81 against angiogenesis, so the upcoming mapping build won't connect them either.
  - The "process" ending would come from the mapping target, because WONT's name for the angiogenesis class is `Angiogenesis process`. So this needs your mapping: `blood vessel formation → Angiogenesis process`.
  - The 3 spelling variants and "formation of blood vessel" would follow it.

Don't worry about losing scope answers when these merge. Scope is decided per concept, so your "in" carries over once the terms are joined.

I can't write either change yet. The scope run is still writing the same file (2,140 of 2,820 batches, about 1.5–2 hours left), and a second writer would overwrite its answers or be overwritten. I'd apply both in the gap before the mapping build.

**Decisions I need from you**
1. **Scope page:** only the 2 real disagreements, or also the old-list reversals?
2. **biologic → biological** as a preferred-word rule, as an exception to your "-ic" convention: yes?
3. **Mapping:** should I also add `blood vessel formation → Angiogenesis process`, and send you any similar paraphrases I find, for review in the same way?

2026-09-28 17:35:04 · Claude
The rules you gave me are coded and tested, but none are applied yet. The scope run is still writing to the same file, so I stopped the chain from starting the next steps and will apply everything once the scope run finishes. Along the way I found and fixed a serious bug the scope run caused.

**The bug**
- The scope run asked the model about the category names themselves ("process", "material entity" and the rest). It answered "out" for all of them.
- Every typing assertion points at one of those names, so that one answer hid all 179,497 of them. The synonym check, the hierarchies and the reasoner check all depend on those categories.
- Nothing raised an error. Two tests failed, which is how I found it.
- Fix: a category name now always counts as in scope, and the scope run no longer asks about categories. It's recorded as rule 52 in `INSTRUCTIONS.md`.
- It's good the chain was stopped: the synonym-mapping build would have run on data without categories.

**Changes made**
- **-ical:** dermatological, oncological, pharmacological, physiological and radiological now win, and biologic(s) becomes biological(s). The product name "XCM BIOLOGIC" is protected.
  - `numerical → numeric` still points the other way. Should I flip it too?
  - Reversing an earlier spelling decision used to be refused as a clash; it now works and keeps the old decision as history.
- **NPWT:** NPWT, VAC, V.A.C., vacuum therapy and vacuum-assisted closure all become "negative pressure wound therapy", inside longer names too. For example, "VAC dressing" becomes "negative pressure wound therapy dressing". That's 106 names and 252 mentions.
  - NPWT itself, with 101 mentions, had never been merged.
  - I left these unchanged: endoscopic/endoluminal vacuum therapy (a gut-leak procedure), bare "vacuum", "negative pressure therapy" and "vacuum sealing drainage". Should the last two also become negative pressure wound therapy?
- **Tests:** 1,709 unit tests pass. The only failure is the check that the exported lists match the register, which waits for the export at the end of the chain. Every new test was confirmed by deliberately breaking the code and watching it fail.

**Approach, strategy and intervention → therapy: your call**
These three words appear in 1,570 names (2,693 mentions). A blanket rename produces nonsense in many of them:
- "search strategy", "coping strategies", "implementation strategies" and "GRADE approach" are study methods, not treatments.
- "surgical approach" and "open approach" mean the access route.
- "diagnostic strategies" would become "diagnostic therapy".
- "prevention strategies" would become "prevention therapy"; prophylaxis isn't therapy in WONT.
- "pre-/post-intervention" are time points.
- "therapeutic strategy" and "treatment strategy" would become "therapeutic therapy" and "treatment therapy".

My proposal:
1. Rename only when the word is the last word of the name.
2. Collapse "therapeutic/treatment + strategy/approach/intervention" to just "therapy".
3. Skip a fixed list of exceptions (search, coping, implementation, GRADE, diagnostic, design, evaluation, the access routes, pre-/post-).
4. Make "prevention/preventive strategy" become "prophylaxis".

OK?

**Words like "massive"**
Yes, this can be done in code, but not with one blanket rule. Too many real terms start with these words: standard deviation, large language model, small molecule, big toe, major/minor amputation, multiple organ failure, traditional Chinese medicine, mild cognitive impairment. Some are also WONT classes: Large wound, Deep wound. I'd split them in two groups:
- **Praise and filler words** (novel, promising, effective, optimal, key, potential, advanced, conventional, standard…; about 2,400 mentions): remove them, with a short exception list you approve.
- **Severity and size words** (massive, severe, mild, moderate, large, extensive, major…; about 1,100 mentions): keep "massive rotator cuff tear" as its own term, recorded as a subtype of "rotator cuff tear". Stripping the word would lose the grade, and your ontology deliberately records grade as a quality.

Want me to draft both word lists and their exceptions as a review page?

**After the scope run finishes**
- **Status:** 2,350 of 2,820 batches, 57,013 terms answered, none failed, about 1.5 hours left. It paused for 10 minutes while saving a checkpoint and then resumed.
- **Sequence:** preview the renames and apply them, add the mapping blood vessel formation → Angiogenesis process, then run the synonym-mapping build, the second opinion and the list export.
- **Credit:** $3.91 remains, which is tight for the build plus the second opinion.

how can i decide on the adjectives
2026-09-28 21:21:57 · Claude
Use the **Adjectives** tab on the Naming Rulings page: https://claude.ai/artifact/KGLqGVcy78ngjRPG7PfE7v

Each row is one word at the start of a name, like "massive …" or "novel …". It shows how many names and mentions start with it, its ten most frequent names, and my proposed choice. For each word, pick one:

- **Strip** (key 1): the word is praise or filler, so it's dropped. "novel therapeutics" becomes "therapeutics".
- **Keep as subtype** (key 2): the word is a grade, so the name stays and is recorded as a kind of the name without the word. "massive rotator cuff tear" becomes a subtype of "rotator cuff tear".
- **Leave alone** (key 3): no rule for this word.

The box next to the buttons is pre-filled with exceptions, names that keep the word whatever you choose (standard deviation, big toe, major amputation…). Add or delete names there, separated by commas.

You can move with J/K and skip with S, as on the other pages. Say "done" when you're finished and I'll turn your choices into naming rules.

For a shortcut, tell me "take the proposals" and I'll apply my proposed choice for all 43 words with the pre-filled exceptions. That means strip for 24 filler words (novel, effective, optimal, key, potential…), keep as subtype for 12 grade words (massive, severe, mild, large, extensive…), and leave alone for 13 (standard, conventional, major, multiple, new…). Or answer only the words you care about and tell me what to do with the rest.

2026-09-29 02:39:26 · Claude
I've started the rest of the rebuild in the background. I'll report when it finishes or if any step fails; the run stops at the first error.

It runs these steps in order:
1. Check every extracted relation against WONT and MILD with the reasoner (ELK).
2. Build the subclass and part-of hierarchies.
3. Check the resulting subclass edges with the reasoner.
4. Run formal concept analysis on the wound subtree. This only proposes candidate superclasses; nothing enters the hierarchy without your acceptance.
5. Write the extended WONT to a separate file, `output/owl/wont_extended.ttl`. The curated WONT file is never touched. The previous version from 8 September is backed up.
6. Run the competency questions against the extended ontology.

Two steps are left out on purpose:
- **Store fill:** it asks the model to invent "missing" attributes and writes them into the evidence store, which should only hold what the abstracts said.
- **Filling the empty cells of the concept-analysis table:** that costs one model call per cell. It's only worth running once you've looked at the lattice.

To your question: no. Of the 65,085 classes in the first extension, 25,951 were out of scope. The extension step was reading the raw extraction file instead of the resolved, scope-filtered version the rest of the pipeline uses. It is the only component that still did that. I fixed it (with a test), rebuilt the extension, and all numbers below are from that rebuild.

**The rebuilt extension**
- **Size:** 35,535 new classes. 34,894 are in scope, 621 have no scope decision yet (e.g. "Width", "Surgical", "P < .05"), and 20 are out on the old exclusion list.
- **Relation targets:** only 37 classes appear solely as the target of a relation. So targets are not what inflates the ontology. Agreed that targets don't need to be classes; they can still serve as attributes for grouping terms in concept analysis.

**Filters, measured, nothing applied**

| Filter | Classes kept |
|---|---:|
| In scope, extracted as a term, same category in every abstract | 34,551 |
| …and mentioned in ≥ 2 abstracts | **6,724** |
| …≥ 3 abstracts | 3,423 |
| …≥ 5 abstracts | 1,570 |
| Linked to a WONT/MILD class by a relation (e.g. has participant some Wound) | 2,230 (921 with the filters above) |
| Placed under a WONT/MILD class as a subclass | **0** |

The zero in the last row is structural. The only subclass statements the literature produces are the category each abstract assigned, like "X is a process". So no extracted term is ever placed under a curated class unless it was mapped onto one; that happened for 827 terms, which don't need new classes. As it stands, the extension is 35,000 flat classes hanging directly under the BFO categories.

**Generic terms.** Word frequency doesn't separate them. Frequent words include Blood, Hand and Eye, which are anatomy, alongside Sex, Female and Race. Of the 6,724, 1,522 are one-word labels. Some are real domain terms (Ulcer, Exudate, Biofilm, Hydrogel, Scar); others are generic (Sex, Female, Tissue, Hospitalization, Type 2). I don't have a rule that tells these apart reliably.

**Placing terms under WONT classes.** One option is the head noun: "Burn Wound" goes under "Wound", "Venous Ulcer" under "Ulcer". Of the 6,724:
- 567 have a head noun that is a WONT/MILD class label, e.g. Burn Wound, Surgical Wound, Hard-To-Heal Wound, Hospital-Acquired Pressure Injury.
- 1,432 have a head noun that is another kept term, e.g. Foot Ulcer → Ulcer, Necrotic Tissue → Tissue, Wound Bed → Bed. Some of these parents are the generic words above.
- 4,725 have no head-noun parent, e.g. Risk Factor, Exudate, Hydrogel, Biofilm, Growth Factor.

The head-noun rule also makes mistakes, e.g. "Wound Size" under "size" and "Diabetes Mellitus Type-2" under "Type 2".

**How I'd proceed**
1. Keep: in scope, extracted as a term, ≥ 2 abstracts, same category everywhere (6,724). Relation targets are used only as concept-analysis attributes, not as classes.
2. You rule on the one-word and generic-looking terms on a review page (about 1,500 rows).
3. Build placement from three sources, each marked by source in the file: head-noun parents, the 1,271 subclass candidates the model adjudications produced, and concept analysis. Then keep only terms whose parent chain reaches a WONT/MILD class.
4. The 947 inconsistently typed terms go to a separate review.

2026-09-29 03:49:06 · Autor
3. why not keep something that relates to a BFO class.
2026-09-29 03:49:23 · Claude
You can. Hanging a term directly under a BFO category is valid BFO, and every kept term already has one. My reasons for wanting more than that:

1. **The category is the weakest evidence the pipeline has.** It comes from one extraction step on each abstract. Where I could compare it with your ontology, it agreed about 86% of the time. The errors were systematic: surgical site infection, dehiscence and sepsis were typed as processes where WONT has material entities. A term whose only parent is its extracted category rests entirely on that step.
2. **It adds no wound structure.** 6,000 classes directly under "process", "material entity" and "quality" is a sorted word list. The reasoner can't classify anything with it, and no competency question benefits from it.
3. **It hides duplicates of your classes.** A term that should sit under, or be, one of your classes looks like a new concept if it only hangs under a category. "Venous Ulcer" would stand beside your "Venous insufficiency wound", and "Hospitalization" beside nothing. Placement under a WONT/MILD class doubles as the check that a term belongs to the wound domain and isn't a twin.

Keeping them has real advantages too: nothing extracted is lost, their relations to other terms stay, and they can be placed later.

**Middle way: two files**
- **Core extension:** terms whose parent chain reaches a WONT/MILD class (head-noun parents, the model's subclass candidates, concept analysis). This is what goes into the release, HermiT and the competency questions.
- **Candidate module:** the terms that only have a BFO category, at least 4,725 of the 6,724. They are kept in a separate file, marked as unplaced, with their category and relations, and importable when you want them. When a later placement step or a curation round finds a WONT parent for one, it moves to the core.

That keeps all of them without the flat list diluting the published ontology. Should I build it that way, or do you want everything in one file under BFO?

Do the middle way

2026-09-29 10:42:58 · Claude
No, the extraction doesn't need to change. My sentence was misleading.

**Every one of the ~6,700 terms already has a BFO class as its parent.** That's the category the extraction assigned (e.g. "Hydrogel is a material entity"), and it's in the files: the 6,368 terms in `wont_candidates.ttl` each sit under their BFO category. By "placed" I meant one level further down: a parent that is one of your WONT/MILD domain classes, such as Burn Wound < Wound. Only 318 terms have that.

**Why so few.** The extraction was designed to ask two things per term and nothing more:
1. its BFO category;
2. whether it is identical to an existing WONT/MILD class. That's how 827 terms were matched to your classes and became no new classes.

It never asked "which WONT class is this term a kind of?". The only places the pipeline gets such parents today are:
- the head-noun rule (315 terms);
- the subclass candidates from the synonym adjudication (3 usable);
- concept analysis (0 so far, because 36% of its table is undecided).

**Why not re-extract.** Re-running extraction would mean reading all 6,029 abstracts again, when the missing piece is a question about the terms, not about the abstracts. A separate placement step can ask it directly:
- For each kept term, take the WONT/MILD classes of a compatible BFO category that are closest to it in embedding similarity, e.g. the top 10.
- Ask the model which of them the term is a kind of, or "none".
- Run the answers through the same category check as the head-noun parents.
- Send uncertain or disputed answers to you on a review page.

That's roughly 6,700 terms in batches of 20: about 335 model calls, around $1. Everything the model places moves from the candidate file into the core, with "model placement" recorded as the source of each edge.

I'd run it after the Category Rulings page, so it doesn't place terms whose category is still in dispute. Should I build it?
2026-09-29 11:00:02 · Autor

2026-09-29 11:00:13 · Claude
The 6,700 is the cut-off you chose: "mentioned in more than one abstract". The rest of the ~30,000 are terms that appear in exactly one abstract.

Of the 35,535 classes the extension created:

| | Terms |
|---|---:|
| In scope and typed into one category | 34,551 |
| …of which in ≥ 2 abstracts (kept now) | 6,686 |
| …of which in exactly 1 abstract (dropped now) | about 27,900 |

The single-mention terms aren't lost. They're still in the evidence store and the register, just not in either output file.

The cut-off guards against one-off phrasings from a single abstract. A good parent is stronger evidence than a second mention, though. A term mentioned once that the model can place under one of your WONT classes, e.g. "Bordered Superabsorbent Wound Dressing" under Wound dressing, is worth keeping. A term mentioned twice that fits nowhere may not be.

So I'd use placement instead of mention count as the gate:
1. Run the placement step over all ~34,500 in-scope, consistently typed terms, not just the 6,700. That's about 1,730 model calls, roughly $4–5.
2. **Core:** every term placed under a WONT/MILD class, however often it's mentioned.
3. **Candidate file:** unplaced terms mentioned in ≥ 2 abstracts, under their BFO category as now.
4. **Left out of both files:** unplaced terms mentioned only once. They stay in the evidence store and can come back if a later run places them.

Should I set it up that way, to run after your Category Rulings?

2026-09-30 01:33:22 · Claude
I've grouped the 1,160 disputed terms by their last word and put the words on a page: https://claude.ai/artifact/2HY2b1T2FzQnts16CDcBFX

- **What counts as the last word:** for names like "loss of tissue" or "time to healing", it's the word before the preposition ("loss", "time"). "Loss of tissue" is a kind of loss, not a kind of tissue.
- **Coverage:** the page lists the 82 words that end at least 3 disputed terms. Together they cover 693 of the 1,160. The rest are one- or two-off words, which stay on the Category Rulings page.
- **Your three rules are already filled in:** … injury → material entity; … infection → process; … inflammation → process.
  - The abstracts disagree with you on infection: 811 times material entity against 664 process.
- **The biggest words after those three:** therapy (63 terms), complication (30), healing (18), method (18), time (16), effect (15), pathway (15), activity (14), trauma (12), reduction (11), ulceration (11), treatment, fracture, control, coverage, change (10 each).
- **Each row shows** how the abstracts typed those terms, with percentages, and example terms.
- **For each word, pick:**
  - one of the three most common categories;
  - any other category from the dropdown;
  - **No rule**, where the word is genuinely mixed (e.g. "change", "loss", "damage" may split between material and process).

These rules can reach further than the disputed terms: the 82 words end about 8,300 terms in total. Once you've answered, I'll first measure how many terms each rule would change beyond the 1,160, and apply only after you've seen those numbers.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Rule 51 architecture:**
     - Concept store = evidence only. ConceptStore.view(mapped, include_rejected) resolves raw → spelling alias → mapping → verdicts → senses.
     - TermMappingAndGate records: spelling, to, pairs (pass1/pass2/curator/refused), gate (curator/pass2/pass1/rule), gate_contested, gate_withdrawn, spelling_reversed, retired.
     - records_of(store) must be used by every reader.
   - **Pass 0 deterministic normalisation.**
     - utils.normalise canonical_vocabulary pipeline: _strip_leading_adjectives → _npwt → _therapy_head → WOUND_MANAGEMENT → POSTSURGICAL → CANONICAL_WORD → CANONICAL_MODIFIER.
     - Rule reversals are handled in apply_renames.
   - **Scope gate:** effective_gate_verdict pools across synonyms. Precedence: curator > pass1/pass2 (contradicting passes fall through) > rule. Blocker (store.scope) reads live.
   - **Models:** SapBERT (cambridgeltl/SapBERT-from-PubMedBERT-fulltext), cosine 0.95. DeepSeek V4 Flash via OpenRouter, temperature 0, JSON mode, BATCH_MAX_TOKENS 32,768.
   - **Tooling:**
     - ELK via ROBOT; HermiT via owlready2.
     - Artifacts with the db capability. Decisions are read with ArtifactData list (out_dir) and applied by scripts with read-back.
     - PlantUML jar at ~/.vscode/extensions/jebbs.plantuml-2.18.1/plantuml.jar, GRAPHVIZ_DOT=/opt/local/bin/dot.
     - Python env: /opt/anaconda3/envs/onto_env/bin/python (first_env has a broken numpy/torch).
   - **Standing rules:**
     - no silent failure;
     - back up to data/backup before writes;
     - read back after writes;
     - mutation-check tests;
     - append rules to INSTRUCTIONS.md;
     - measured, not estimated.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **Thesis:** `Ontology/Thesis/`.
     - chapters 00_front_matter … 10_references: EN abstract, DE Zusammenfassung; MIRO chapters for MILD (ch4) and WONT (ch5); engineering (ch6); results (ch7, with interim markers); discussion; references with [verify] markers.
     - appendices: A_B_miro_checklists.md (levels corrected from the MIRO PDF: D.3 should; E.7/E.10/F.*/G.5 must); C_competency_questions.md (generated); D_prompts.md (stub).

     - apply_renames reversal branch: an alias whose target is renamed onto itself moves to `spelling_reversed`; report "spellings reversed".

**Five Signature Words rulings to double-check** before I apply anything. A word rule changes every term that ends in that word, which is up to about 8,300 terms in total:
- **pathway, survival → process boundary.** Boundaries are instantaneous, so "flap survival" and "graft survival" would stop being processes. Process may be what you meant.
- **pain, dehiscence, rupture → immaterial entity.** In BFO that category covers holes, cavities and surfaces. Pain is usually treated as a quality or a process, and dehiscence and rupture as processes. Rupture's result, the opening itself, could fairly be an immaterial entity, so this may be deliberate.

When you've finished the Signature Words, I'll count exactly how many terms each word rule changes, show you those numbers, and only apply the rules once you've seen them. Anything the word rules don't settle stays on the Category Rulings page.

- **93 terms match the rule.** 46 you had already put out of scope yourself; I put out the 45 that were still open, including TEN, TEWL, TLR, LIPUS, FREMS, HBO, MI, US, PT/OT and 20CRW.

The rebuild finished, but the result isn't usable yet, so I haven't run the split or the placement on it.

**What went wrong:** ELK found 7,361 contradictory (unsatisfiable) classes. Because of that, the reasoner check set aside 20,844 relations and 7,076 terms; on 28 September it set aside none. Nothing is deleted: those terms stay in the store, and the set-aside relations are stored as reversible verdicts that the next reasoner run lifts and re-checks. The extended ontology was built from that reduced data, so I'm not using it. The competency questions still answer 49 of 49.

**Likely cause:** it's a chain reaction from a few starting points, not 7,000 independent errors. Most of the contradictory classes depend on others in the set. Among the ones that look like starting points are WONT/MILD classes with no relations of their own, such as "Wound dressing process" and MILD's tendency class. So a curated class has itself become contradictory, and everything related to it follows. My leading guess is the category decisions I added: a term mapped onto a curated class may now carry a category that contradicts that class. For example, "wound dressing" with your "dressing → material entity" rule, mapped onto "Wound dressing process". Before the rulings, such terms were typed inconsistently and simply skipped by the check.

**Now:** a search is running in the background to find the smallest set of relations that makes MILD's tendency class contradictory. Once I know the exact cause, I'll fix it so the ontology's own category wins whenever a term maps onto a WONT/MILD class, as you said. Then I'll re-run the reasoner check and the rest.

2026-10-01 02:18:13 · Claude
It's running again, and I found and fixed the cause.

**Cause:** one abstract says something about the bare word "tendency": "tendency has realization therapy". Because "tendency" is the name of MILD's own tendency class, the reasoner check stated that sentence as a rule about the whole class. That was harmless while "therapy" had no settled category. Once your term ruling made "therapy" an information entity, it became a contradiction, since a realization has to be a process. That made the tendency class itself contradictory. It then spread to every term typed as a tendency (all the risk factors) and to the WONT/MILD classes that mention a tendency, such as Abscess, Neoplasm and Wound dehiscence process. In total that was 7,361 contradictory classes, which is why 20,844 relations were set aside.

My earlier guess (a term mapped onto a curated class with a conflicting category) was wrong. The category decisions were only the trigger.

**Fix:** a relation whose subject is a category class itself (tendency, process, disposition, …) is now held back by name before reasoning. It is listed in the report rather than turned into a rule about the category. A new test checks this, and the test fails if the fix is removed; the other 23 reasoner-check tests still pass. I also added this to rule 52 in INSTRUCTIONS.md.

2026-10-01 04:43:36 · Claude
The search found the second cause, and I've fixed it. The rebuild and the full test suite are now running again.

**Cause:** one sentence from one abstract: "healing has as process boundary at end completely healed". That requires the end of healing to be an instant (a process boundary), but "completely healed" is typed a quality. The sentence was always wrong, but it was harmless while "healing" had no settled category and was skipped by the reasoner check. With "healing" now a process, the check asserted it and "healing" became contradictory. The contradiction then spread along ordinary, correct relations:
- impaired wound healing is part of healing;
- gel participates in impaired wound healing;
- infection has participant gel;
- pathogen participates in infection;
- material disease entity is causally influenced by pathogen, which drags in MILD.

That added up to 7,163 contradictory classes, and the check's rule of dropping every relation that touches a contradictory term took out 20,777 relations.

**Fix:** before reasoning on the whole batch, the reasoner check now tests each relation on its own against the ontology, in a single extra ELK run. A relation that is contradictory by itself, like the healing sentence, is set aside on its own and listed in the report. Its neighbours, like "impaired wound healing part of healing", stay in.

**Tests:**
- **New ELK test:** it reproduces the healing case. It fails if the fix is removed and passes with it.
- **Existing tests:** the reasoner-check tests pass (39 + 6).

I also added both night's failures, the tendency one and this one, to rule 52 in INSTRUCTIONS.md.

**Running now:** the full rebuild and the complete test suite. If the number of set-aside relations drops back near the 28 September level, I'll run the core/candidate split and the placement for the new full names.

2026-10-01 13:19:47 · Claude
I went through the sandbox myself. Most answers are right, but merging them as they stand would put too many wrong parents into the core ontology. I judged about 300 answers by hand, and the error rate depends strongly on two signals I can compute for every row, so most of the merge can be sorted mechanically.

**The two signals:**
- **Shared wording:** whether the term and the suggested parent share a word.
- **Similarity:** how close the embedding model puts the two names (0 to 1).

| Group | Rows | Wrong in my sample | Examples of errors |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Is a", parent shares a word with the term or similarity ≥ 0.65 | 7,811 | about 1 in 10 (5 of 50) | edema formation → *Exudation process*; Wagner grade 3 diabetic foot osteomyelitis → *Diabetic foot ulcer* |
| "Is a", no shared word and low similarity | 10,044 | about 1 in 3 (15 of 50) | donor site → *Wound base*; vascular invasion → *angiogenesis*; caspase-8 → *pnictogen molecular entity*; bowel preparation → *material processing* |
| "Same as", similarity ≥ 0.75 | 346 | none in my sample | impaired wound healing = *Impaired wound healing process*; Wagner classification = *Wagner and Anderson wound classification* |
| "Same as", similarity < 0.75 | 258 | about 1 in 5 | Kfgsi = *Karnofsky score*; narrowing of artery = *vasoconstriction*; skin margin = *Wound edge* |
| Parent not among the offered classes, or "is a" with no parent | 143 | not usable | — |

**Other patterns:**
- **Dumping-ground parents.** A few classes collect wrong placements:
  - *material processing* (bowel preparation, "addition of PRP").
  - Chemistry classes that describe structure only, such as *pnictogen* or *chalcogen molecular entity*: caspase-8 or kaempferol under them is technically true but useless.
  - *categorical label*.
- **Leftover extraction noise.** Phrases like "promote wound healing", "developed a soft tissue infection" or "supplemented" are verb phrases, not concepts. They come from extraction and still need a cleanup pass.
- **Placements that are fine:** cells under *cell*, dressings under *Wound dressing*, anatomy under *gross anatomical part*, and grading systems under the classification scales.

**What I recommend for the merge:**
1. **Synonyms:** map the 346 "same as" answers with similarity ≥ 0.75. The other 258 become candidates.
2. **Core ontology:** the "is a" answers in the better group that appear in at least two abstracts (1,963). Before merging, a second yes/no check by the model on each one, estimated at about $0.30 of the $2.18 left. That should catch most of the roughly 10% that are wrong.
3. **Candidate module:** everything else, which is about 16,000 terms. The suggested parent is kept as a note, not asserted as a parent. The two dumping-ground parents and the structure-only chemistry classes are never kept as suggestions.
4. **Dropped:** the 143 unusable answers.

Decision for you: should I run the yes/no check on the 1,963 before they go into the core? The alternative is to merge them directly and accept roughly 10% wrong parents there.

Zusammenfassung des bisherigen Verlaufs (bei Kontextkomprimierung erzeugt)
2026-10-01 13:37:30 · Systemmeldung oder Nachricht einer anderen Claude-Sitzung (nicht vom Autor)
This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - **Overall project:** a wound ontology pipeline (WONT addition on top of MILD/BFO) plus a thesis. The ground truth (`ground_truth_final.owl`, WONT) stays unaltered; what we build is the WONT *addition* (extension files), not changes to WONT.
   - **Category rulings:**
     - Signature Words head-word rules apply to **disputes only** (terms whose abstracts disagree irreconcilably).
     - The user's rechecks: pressure→quality, pain→quality, pathway/survival→process, procedure→process. "time" → the new category **temporal region** (BFO_0000008).
     - Term rulings outrank head rules. For WONT/MILD class names "there is no dispute, the ontology decides".
   - **Abbreviations:** "no abbreviations ever", "no guessing".
     - Strict typographic detection.
     - Rename only to a full form defined in the abstracts.
     - User's final rule: no suggestion → out of scope; genuinely different suggestions → out of scope; synonyms or spelling variants → keep.
     - MRI→magnetic resonance imaging, SSc→systemic sclerosis, AKI→acute kidney injury.
     - "postoperative drainage volume" stays quality.
   - **Placement sandbox review**, the user's latest instructions:
     - **(1)** Explain why "same" answers were not caught by the mapping. Explained: 234 of the 346 had SapBERT 0.75–0.95, below the 0.95 mapping threshold; about 90 had GO/CHEBI/PATO module targets, and only WONT/MILD labels are mapping targets (anchors); 15 were rejected by the category guard when categories were disputed. This has not yet been reported to the user.
     - **(2)** "do it": run the model's yes/no check on the selected placements.
     - **(3)** Check the remaining ~16k again, "maybe with a better model, maybe you check all of them yourself". I am doing this myself.
     - **(4)** Drop the unusable 143 answers ("thanks").
   - Standing rules:
     - no silent failure;
     - back up before writes;
     - read back after writes;
     - append rules to INSTRUCTIONS.md;
     - measure, don't estimate.
   - The user's email is used only for identification.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Term dict (TermMappingAndGate) records:**
     - existing fields: spelling alias, to, pairs (pass1/pass2/curator/refused), gate (curator/pass2/pass1/rule), relations (cell rejections), senses;
     - NEW `category` section {verdict, source ("term ruling" | "head rule: <word>"), by, note, when, history}.
   - **ConceptStore.view():** now applies decided categories per abstract via `_apply_categories`.
     - A category cell at or below the decision stands; others are replaced by the decision at the max value.
     - Untyped mentions stay untyped; non-category is_a targets are untouched.
     - When mapped=False, the lookup also goes through the mapped resolver.
   - **Reasoner gate (ELK via ROBOT):**
     - relations whose subject is a BFO/MILD category class are held back;
     - a `_locally_inconsistent` pre-check asserts each relation on fresh probe copies of its subject and object (category + grounding), reasons once, and holds back relations that are wrong on their own;
     - a `robot explain` timeout is reported instead of crashing.
   - **Abbreviation expansion:** Schwartz & Hearst plus a word-by-word `spells()` check.
   - **Placement:** DeepSeek via OpenRouter, sandbox `data/review/placement_2026-09-29.json`; filtered current view `output/placement_current_2026-10-01.json`.
   - **Split files:** `split_extension.py` writes `output/owl/wont_extended_core.ttl` (placed + WONT import) and `wont_candidates.ttl`. These are separate files; the ground truth is unchanged.
   - **DataManager:** saves timestamped files (minute resolution), loads latest by name sort, keeps about 10.
   - **Environment:** Python /opt/anaconda3/envs/onto_env/bin/python; project root `/Users/Strubbelig/Library/Mobile Documents/com~apple~CloudDocs/Forschung/Ontology/Wound ontology`.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **src/wound_ontology/mappers/term_mapping_and_gate.py**
     - Added `CATEGORY = "category"` and `TERM_RULING = "term ruling"`; added "category" to DECISION_SECTIONS.
     - Added `categories()`.
     - Added `set_category(term, verdict, *, source, by="curator", note="")`, which returns "set" / "same" / "kept" and raises on an unknown category.
     - `_absorb` merges category: a term ruling wins; conflicts are reported in `report["category conflicts"]`.
     - The empty-record check keeps CATEGORY.
     - The apply_renames report includes "category conflicts".
   - **src/wound_ontology/store/concept_store.py**
     - Added `_category_chain` and `_apply_categories`.
     - view() computes `cats`, `via_map`, `decided_for(final, raw)` and applies them before senses.
     - The extraction message now distinguishes an EMPTY abstract table from "already in store".
     - "Failed PMID" prints the exception type.
   - **src/wound_ontology/llm/schema_builder.py:** added an ANCHORS entry ("temporal region", BFO_0000008, parent occurrent) and BFO_SKELETON "BFO_0000008": "BFO_0000003". Regenerated `extraction_models.py` and `config/extraction_schema.yaml` (TemporalRegion added; OCCUPIES_TEMPORAL_REGION now process boundary → temporal region).
   - **src/wound_ontology/reasoning/skeleton.py:** BFO_SKELETON, BFO_LABELS and BFO_DISJOINT ("BFO_0000015", "BFO_0000008") added.
   - **src/wound_ontology/reasoning/gate.py**
     - `_explain` try/except TimeoutExpired.
     - In check(): the on-category hold-back (prints "[ReasonerGate] N relation(s) whose subject is a BFO/MILD category class held back").
     - `local = self._locally_inconsistent(ents, rels) if rels else []`, which holds back relations that are wrong on their own.
     - New `_locally_inconsistent` method (probe classes `BATCH_NS probe_s_n` / `probe_o_n`).
     - Backup of the old file in `data/backup/category_rulings_2026-09-29/gate.py`.
   - **config/category_head_rules.py (new):** HEAD_CATEGORY (78 heads, including the rechecks and time → temporal region); HEAD_OUT = {"involvement", "ssi"}.
   - **scripts/measure_head_rules.py (new):** read-only measurement → `output/head_rules_measure_2026-09-29.json`.
   - **scripts/apply_category_rulings.py (new)**
     - Contains TERM_NOTES, NOTE_CATEGORY (material entity, immaterial entity, quality, role, realizable entity, continuant, plan specification/plan/classification result → information entity, time interval → temporal region) and TERM_MAPS (AKI, MRI, SSc).
     - Applies term rulings, head rules (disputes only), outs, maps and parents.
     - The backup is timestamped; it re-exports gate lists, reads back, and checks the view.
     - "Still disputed" excludes WONT/MILD labels.
   - **scripts/expand_abbreviations.py (new):** `definitions()`, `spells()`, `_sh_match`; buckets expand / unspelled / ambiguous / undefined / decided; writes with one save; single-abstract undefined → out. Report: `output/abbreviation_expansion_2026-09-30.json`.
   - **scripts/apply_abbreviation_rulings.py (new):** KEEP dict (36 ambiguous kept), UNSPELLED_OUT (17), UNSPELLED_FIX (PPE → personal protective equipment, TLC-NOSF, PAMCAI). Applied: 119 mapped, 165 out.
   - **scripts/p4_place_terms.py**
     - Placement model fields Optional; null term skipped.
     - `--batch` and `--timeout` options.
     - OUT fixed to `placement_2026-09-29.json`.
     - The budget stop only fires when batches remain.
   - **scripts/p4_verify_placements.py (new):** yes/no check of selected placements (is_a with ≥2 abstracts and shared word or SapBERT ≥0.65; same with SapBERT ≥0.75) = 2,309 rows; writes `data/review/placement_verify_2026-10-01.json`; budget 0.8.
   - **Tests:**
     - `tests/test_category_decisions.py` (new, 14 tests, mutation-checked);
     - `tests/test_gate_store_relations.py` (added the category-class hold-back test; stub sets `gate._locally_inconsistent = lambda ents, rels: []`);
     - `tests/test_reasoner_gate_elk.py` (added the healing test, mutation-checked).
   - **INSTRUCTIONS.md:**
     - rule 57 (decided category; head rule settles only a dispute; temporal region);
     - rule 58 (no abbreviations: typographic rule, Schwartz-Hearst plus spelling check, the curator's three-way rule; measured 825 renamed, 726 out, 5 already ruled, of 1,556);
     - rule 52 extended (category-class relations held back; locally inconsistent relations).
   - **config/whitelist.py:** removed the keyword entries "glycemic control", "patient safety", "histology", "shear", "vascular compromise" (backups in `data/backup/category_rulings_2026-09-29/`).
   - **Memory:** `feedback_no_abbreviations.md` added and indexed in MEMORY.md.

     - Signature Words (2HY2b1T2FzQnts16CDcBFX): Show filter, "✓ ruled", retry load; rechecked docs updated in its db.

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **"Hide decided" defaulted on** (Category Rulings), so the user thought their rulings were lost. Replaced it with a Show select (all / undecided / mine) and a "✓ ruled" label.
   - **The page said "could not read earlier decisions".** Added retries, the real error message, and a separate draw-error message.

   - **disease/morbidity mapped to "material disease entity"** left the view disputed. The target is now also given the category.
   - **test_gate_lists_are_a_view drift** after gate writes. Call `p.export_gate_lists()` in the scripts.
   - **test_curation_list_shape conflicts** (whitelist keywords vs new outs). Removed the keyword entries after checking that no longer term depended on them.
   - **expand_abbreviations used gt.add** (saves 45 MB on every call); killed mid-write, which left a truncated file. Moved it to `data/quarantine/…TRUNCATED…`, restored from backup, and switched to a single save.
   - **Restored copy named with a future timestamp (20-12)** shadowed the new save, causing a false "did not land". Removed it.
   - **p4_derive crashed on `robot explain` timeout.** Fixed with the try/except.
   - **Massive quarantine (20,844 relations, 7,361 unsat).**
     - Cause 1: "tendency has realization therapy" asserted on the MILD tendency class. Fixed with the category-class hold-back.
     - Cause 2 (found by ddmin): "healing has as process boundary at end completely healed" (a quality). Fixed with `_locally_inconsistent`.
     - Final result: 14 relations quarantined, 25,510 committed, 49/49 CQ.
   - **Placement failures** (timeouts, null term). Optional fields; smaller batches.
   - **Integration test test_extract_to_cache:**
     - fails standalone because there is no PubMed table;
     - in sequence it fails on LLM provider timeouts;
     - not caused by our changes. The non-integration suite passed: 1,802 passed, 1 xfailed.
