2026-10-01 13:54:54 · Systemmeldung oder Nachricht einer anderen Claude-Sitzung (nicht vom Autor)
This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - **Project:** a wound ontology pipeline (a WONT addition on top of MILD/BFO) plus a thesis. The ground truth WONT (`ground_truth_final.owl`) stays unaltered. We build only the WONT addition files (`output/owl/wont_extended_core.ttl` and `wont_candidates.ttl`).
   - **Latest user instructions on the placement sandbox:**
     - "first, what do you mean with going into the core? the ground truth stays unaltered we just built the WONT addition, not WONT ground truth."
     - "1. these should have been taken care of by the mapping. why did they slip?" Explanation prepared, not yet reported:
       - 234 of the 346 "same" answers had SapBERT 0.75–0.95, below the 0.95 mapping threshold;
       - about 90 had GO/CHEBI/PATO module targets, which are not mapping anchors (only WONT/MILD labels are);
       - 15 were rejected by the category guard when categories were disputed.
     - "2. do it": the model's yes/no verify of the selected placements (running).
     - "3. these have to be checked again, maybe with a better model, maybe you check all of them yourself": I am checking all ~16k rows myself.
     - "4. thanks": drop the 143 unusable answers.
   - **Standing rules:**
     - no silent failure;
     - back up before writes, read back after writes;
     - append design rules to INSTRUCTIONS.md;
     - measure, don't estimate;
     - "no abbreviations ever / no guessing";
     - Signature Words head rules apply to disputes only; for WONT/MILD names "the ontology decides".
     - The user's email is used only for identification.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Term dict (TermMappingAndGate):** carries a category section. `ConceptStore.view()` applies the decided categories.
   - **ReasonerGate:** ELK via ROBOT. It holds back relations on category classes and relations that are locally inconsistent.
   - **Placement:** done by DeepSeek via OpenRouter.
     - Sandbox: `data/review/placement_2026-09-29.json`.
     - Current view: `output/placement_current_2026-10-01.json`, with 33,657 rows (17,995 is_a / 607 same / 15,055 none).
   - **Verify script:** `scripts/p4_verify_placements.py`.
     - It selects is_a rows with ≥2 abstracts and either a shared word or SapBERT ≥0.65, plus same rows with SapBERT ≥0.75: 2,309 rows.
     - Batches of 20, `max_tokens=8000`, rounds of 40 batches; `--budget-usd 0.8`, `--concurrency 4`.
     - Writes `data/review/placement_verify_2026-10-01.json` and is resumable.
   - **Self-check inputs:** `data/review/placement_selfcheck_2026-10-01/rows.json` holds 16,150 rows.
     - The chunks `chunk_00.txt` … `chunk_26.txt` hold 600 rows each, in the format `n|term|cat|< or = parent|1:alt;...;4:alt`.
   - **Self-check verdict files `verdicts_XX.txt`:**
     - Space-separated tokens `n:0` (no parent), `n:1`–`n:4` (choose that alternative) or `n:I` (demote "same" to is_a).
     - Unlisted rows are accepted.
   - **Recurring judgement conventions:**
     - Reject dumping-ground parents: material component separation, material processing, material information bearer, physiological role, regulation of biological quality / body fluid levels / tube diameter / anatomical structure size (for interventions), peptide zwitterion, qualitative, chemistry fallback classes.
     - Procedures placed under biological-process parents get 0.
     - Brain and spinal injuries under Traumatic wound or Wound get 0.
     - Risk scales (Braden, Norton) under Wound classification scale or grade get 0.
     - Anatomical regions under spatial region get the "immaterial anatomical entity" alternative.
     - Microenvironments get 0.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **Verdict files written so far** in `data/review/placement_selfcheck_2026-10-01/`:
     - `verdicts_00.txt` – `verdicts_06.txt` were written in earlier sessions.

2026-10-01 14:28:42 · Claude
Every placement answer has now had a second check. The merged result is a sandbox file; nothing is in the term dict or the WONT addition yet, and the WONT ground truth was never touched.

**"Core."** By "core" I meant `output/owl/wont_extended_core.ttl`, the classes the WONT addition asserts. The other addition file is `wont_candidates.ttl`. Calling it "the core" was misleading; I'll call it the WONT addition from now on.

**1. Why the "same" answers slipped past the mapping.** My earlier draft explanation was incomplete. Measured across all 607 "same" answers:
- **557 sit below the 0.95 mapping threshold:** 296 between 0.75 and 0.95, and 261 below 0.75. The mapping never saw them as candidates.
- **33 of the 50 at ≥ 0.95 point at GO, CHEBI, PATO and other module classes.** Those labels are not mapping anchors; only WONT and MILD labels are.
- **The 15 at ≥ 0.95 that point at WONT classes break down like this:**
  - 3 were refused by the mapping's own checks ("wounded skin" vs "Skin wound" judged different).
  - 9 never reached a verdict. I haven't traced why yet. The likely cause is that the clustering only merges when every member of a cluster is ≥ 0.95 to the label.
  - 3 hit a real bug, which affects 18 labels in total (next point).

**A bug in the mapping.** 18 WONT/MILD labels are mapped onto a store spelling instead of the other way round. Examples: "Wound treatment process" → "wound therapy", and "Impaired wound healing process" → "impaired wound healing". This breaks rule 36 (the ontology's label is the canonical), and the WONT class gets folded into the store term.
- Cause: a label only counts as an anchor if no abstract wrote it verbatim. Once an abstract writes it, corpus frequency picks the store spelling instead.
- The existing repair (`enforce_canonical_policy`) only fixes case and plural differences, so "X process" → "X" is never corrected.
- I have not fixed it. Your call: I'd fix the 18 term-dict records directly (back up, swap the direction, read back), and fix the anchor rule in code before the next mapping rebuild.

**2. Verify run (model yes/no on 2,309 rows).** All 2,309 are answered, for $0.35 in total.
- is_a: 1,510 yes, 453 no.
- same: 203 yes, 143 no.
- 7 batches hit the token cap and were cut off. Their 140 rows were rerun in batches of 5 with no failures. I added a `--batch-size` option to the script for this.
- The reasons I sampled hold up, for example "sinus tract is not necessarily a fistula" and "systemic antimicrobial therapy includes non-antibiotic agents".

**3. My check of the other 16,150 rows.** All 27 chunks are done.
- 4,100 parents refused (25%).
- 79 replaced by one of the offered alternatives; each resolved to exactly one class.
- 89 "same" answers demoted to is_a.
- One mistake was caught and fixed: an earlier write had the wrong row list for rows 14800–14999 in `verdicts_24.txt`.

**4.** The 143 unusable answers are dropped.

**Merged result** ([placement_merged_2026-10-01.json](Wound ontology/data/review/placement_merged_2026-10-01.json), written by [merge_placement_checks.py](Wound ontology/scripts/merge_placement_checks.py) and read back):

|---|---|

| Synonym of an existing class | 310 |
| Candidate (parent refused; it is kept as "suggested" for you to see) | 4,696 |
| Unplaced (the model found no parent) | 15,055 |
| Dropped | 143 |

I added this as rule 59 in INSTRUCTIONS.md.

2026-10-01 21:59:11 · Claude
The code fix for the reversed labels is done and tested. The write to the term dict is not: the permission check blocked it as a change to a shared resource, so the term dict is unchanged.

**What I checked.** I looked at each of the 18 labels that point at a store spelling:
- **16 point the wrong way, and fixing them is clear-cut.** Each pair has `same_class` from both adjudication passes; only the direction is wrong. For three of them you had kept the correct direction yourself and a rebuild flipped it back: "Wound healing by primary / secondary / tertiary intention" → the WONT label.
- **2 point that way because you chose it.** These are "painful" → "painful condition" and "threshold value" → "threshold". The fix leaves them alone and reports them.
- **The category check refused none of the 16.**

**What changed in the code.** The canonical-label policy that every mapping build runs now has a fourth rule: "a label is never mapped away". When a WONT/MILD label points at a store spelling, it flips the entry. The store spelling, and everything mapped onto it, now maps onto the label. A direction you chose is kept and reported. There are four new tests in `tests/test_canonical_policy.py`, all passing. Disabling the flip makes two of them fail, so they do catch the bug. I recorded this as rule 60 in INSTRUCTIONS.md.

**Why a separate script.** Running the full policy with `--apply` would also carry out six pending changes nobody has decided on. Five are refusals by the category check, e.g. "SSI" → "Surgical site infection" would be unmapped, and one is a re-spelling. [apply_label_restore.py](Wound ontology/scripts/apply_label_restore.py) applies only the 16 flips. It takes a backup, writes, then reloads the file and checks every entry.

**Decision for you:** either run it yourself, or allow it and tell me to go ahead:
```
/opt/anaconda3/envs/onto_env/bin/python -u scripts/apply_label_restore.py --write
```

**Something to check.** "wound healing" → "impaired wound healing" is marked "kept" from the review page on 2026-09-24. As it stands, every "wound healing" mention reads as *impaired* wound healing. It looks like a mis-click, but it's your decision, so I left it.

After the flips are in, I'll write the placements into the term dict and rebuild the WONT addition.

|---|---|---|

**Short answers:** yes, it can be reversed. Yes, the placements are checked, except the "no parent" rows. And ELK finds no inconsistency in them.

**1. Is writing placements into the term dict reversible?** Yes, two ways:
- **Whole file:** the term dict is saved as a new timestamped file on every write, and the older versions stay in `data/cache`. On top of that, every write script makes an explicit backup first (today's are in `data/backup/term_mapping_before_*`). Restoring the previous file undoes any write.
- **Per term:** I'd store placements the way your category rulings are stored. Each record would get its own placement section saying where it was placed, by which check, and when. A later change keeps the earlier decision under history. So you could withdraw one placement, or all the ones from a single check, without touching anything else.
- **The ontology follows:** the WONT addition is built from the term dict. Rebuilding after a reversal reverses the ontology too.
- **Not built yet:** that placement section doesn't exist yet. I'd design it before writing anything, possibly as part of the prior work you mentioned.

**2. Are the placements checked?** Every "is a" and "same as" answer has been read by me, row by row: 18,465 rows in total.
- 2,313 of these also had the model's yes/no; I disagreed with it on 202.
- 15,058 rows where the model found no parent have not been checked by anyone. They aren't placed, so they can't make the ontology wrong. But some probably have a correct parent the model missed.

**3. What does ELK say?** Nothing is inconsistent.
- **What I tested:** in a sandbox, I asserted all 13,778 placements and synonyms on the skeleton (WONT, MILD and BFO, including BFO's disjointness axioms). Each term went in as a class under its ruled category and under its parent, or equivalent to its class for synonyms.
- **Result:** 0 placements are unsatisfiable, and no existing WONT/MILD class becomes unsatisfiable.
- **The test can fail:** when I injected a process under `Wound`, ELK named it.
- **One row couldn't be fully tested:** "hydrogen" under CHEBI's `hydrogen molecular entity`, because that class isn't in the skeleton and has no recorded category. It is a chemical, so it's consistent.
- **What ELK can't catch:** it only finds placements that contradict their category. A parent that is consistent but wrong is what the row-by-row reading was for.

**The inconsistency issues you remember** came from two other places, not from placements:
- **Relations from the abstracts:** in late September, a few wrongly typed relations made hub classes unsatisfiable. For example, "therapy" became unsatisfiable once it was ruled an information entity, and that cascade made 7,361 classes unsatisfiable. The gate now holds back a relation on a category class, and any relation that is wrong on its own, before reasoning.
- **HermiT on the disease proposal:** it stalled on the DOID module's equivalence axioms back in June.

Neither problem applies to placements. The full reasoner run with HermiT on the WONT addition is still to come, after FCA.

I added the ELK check and its result to rule 59. The script is [elk_check_placements.py](Wound ontology/scripts/elk_check_placements.py).

**Decision for you:** should I design the placement section now, as the reversible way to write placements, and show it to you before anything is written?
2026-10-02 01:03:05 · Autor
What do you mean with placement? Another glossary term here?
2026-10-02 01:03:15 · Claude
Yes, it's my own working word, not an established term. In ontology language, "placement" means two things:

- **Assigning a superclass.** I decide which existing WONT/MILD class an extracted term is a subclass of. For example, "silver alginate dressing" is a subclass of `Alginate containing wound dressing`. In the ontology that becomes an `rdfs:subClassOf` axiom.
- **Recognising a synonym.** The term names a class that already exists. For example, "pressure sore" is `Pressure ulcer`. In the ontology that becomes a synonym label on the existing class, not a new class.

What I called the "placement step" is the model proposing a superclass or a synonym for each term from its ten nearest classes. The "checks" were the reading of those proposals, by me row by row and by the model's yes/no. And a "placement section" would just be a field in the term's record in the term dict. It would hold the chosen superclass or synonym target, who decided it, and when.

The superclass part is close to what is often called subsumption (or classification), and the synonym part to mapping. I'll call them "superclass" and "synonym" from now on. If you'd rather have different wording, especially for the thesis, tell me and I'll rename it in INSTRUCTIONS.md and the scripts.

2026-10-02 01:24:16 · Claude
Let me check how the term dict records subclass judgements today before I answer, because the answer depends on it:
2026-10-02 01:24:17 · Werkzeug Bash:
2026-10-02 01:24:20 · Werkzeug Bash:
2026-10-02 01:24:42 · Claude
The placement results don't need a new dict, and they don't patch the mapping-and-gate dict either. They belong in the term dict as a third kind of judgement on term–class pairs, next to the two adjudication passes that already live there. Right now they sit outside the architecture.

**How it fits together today:**
```
concept store (evidence, never changed)
        │
        ▼  view = store read through the term dict
term dict (TermMappingAndGate), one record per term:
   to        → the class/name it maps onto (synonym = mapping)
   gate      → in / out of scope
   category  → the ruled BFO category
   pairs     → judgements on (term, class) pairs:
                pass1 / pass2 / curator: same_class | different | narrower | broader
```

The pairs already hold subclass judgements. Pass 1 called 751 term–class pairs `narrower` ("the term is a subclass of this class"), and you marked 23 yourself. `subsumption_candidates()` collects those, and `split_extension.py` turns them into the `subClassOf` axioms of the WONT addition.

**Where the placement results are now:** only in sandbox files in `data/review` (the model's proposals, the two checks, the merged result). That was deliberate, because you said on 29 Sept "don't merge the answers yet". But it means the term dict, the view and the build know nothing about them.

**Where they would go:**
- **A subclass** becomes a pair judgement `narrower` on (term, superclass), recorded under its own source key alongside pass1, pass2 and curator, with who checked it. It doesn't change the view: the term keeps its name in the store view. It only feeds the hierarchy (FCA, then the build), through `subsumption_candidates()`. That function currently reads only pass1 and pass2, so it would need to read the new source too.
- **A synonym** becomes a pair judgement `same_class` and also a mapping (`to` = the class). This is the only part that changes what the view looks like, because the term's mentions merge into the existing class. It is exactly what the mapping step does, so it goes through the same guards: refusals, the category guard, and the canonical-label rules.
- **A refused superclass** becomes a pair judgement saying it was refused. It is kept, not deleted (rule 33), so nobody asks the same question again.

**Reversibility, concretely:** every one of these judgements carries its source. Withdrawing all placement results means removing that one source key from the pairs, plus the 332 mappings it set, which are themselves marked with their source. Everything from the mapping passes and from you stays untouched.

So the placement step is really a third adjudication of pairs, with a model proposing and a reader checking. It should land where the other two passes land.

The review page is up: **[Superclasses and Synonyms](https://claude.ai/artifact/QB3gqPiQvaJDGWhxoLD2aN)**. It works like the Category Rulings page: your decisions save as you click.

**Synonyms tab (332 terms):** grouped by the class they would merge into, e.g. the 17 spellings that become `Ulcus cruris venosum`.
- Nothing here counts as accepted until you click. You can accept or refuse each term, or use "Accept all" / "Refuse all" for a group.
- Clicking a pressed button again withdraws it.
- The progress bar and counter track these.

**Superclasses tab (13,446 terms):** grouped by superclass, largest first (`immaterial anatomical entity` 477, `Surgical treatment process` 312, …), with a source label (WONT, GO, CHEBI, …).
- Everything here counts as accepted unless you refuse a single term or a whole group.
- Each group shows its 8 most-mentioned terms; "Show all" opens the rest.

**What each term shows:** how many abstracts mention it and who decided it:
- "Claude": my row-by-row reading;
- "model + Claude": the model's yes/no and I agree;
- "Claude over the model": I overruled the model;
- "Claude's parent": I picked a different superclass from the alternatives;
- "Claude: subclass, not synonym": the model said synonym, I made it a subclass.

**Filters:** search finds a class or a term, there's a source filter, and "Show" can narrow to undecided groups or to groups with refusals.

Tell me when you're done. I'll then read your decisions from the page and write the results into the term dict:
- **subclasses** become superclass judgements under their own source;
- **synonyms** become mappings;
- **refusals** become kept refusals.

The script takes a backup first and reloads the file to confirm every decision landed. It has the same reversal path as before, and FCA comes after that.

2026-10-02 14:38:50 · Claude
These three are all the same kind of fix: a naming rule that has to reach inside compound names, not just whole names. Here's what each would do, measured on the current in-scope names, and what I need from you.

**1. Pressure injury → pressure ulcer.** 161 names contain "pressure injury" (694 mentions).
- 20 would merge into an existing "pressure ulcer" name, e.g. "sacral pressure injury" (6) → "sacral pressure ulcer" (6), "hospital-acquired pressure injury" (40) → "hospital-acquired pressure ulcer" (7), "pressure injury risk" (23) → "pressure ulcer risk" (9).
- The other 141 just change their wording.
- `Pressure ulcer` is the WONT label, so the direction is fixed. **I'd apply this as stated.**

**2. illness / disorder / pathology / disease / condition / dysfunction.** These words appear in 494 names. Swapping them makes 28 groups (67 names) merge, e.g. "skin condition" + "skin disease" + "skin dysfunction" + "skin pathology", or "autoimmune disorder" + "autoimmune disease" + "autoimmune condition". Two things I need from you:
- **Which word should win?** WONT/MILD labels use "disease" 45 times and "disorder" 17 times, and the abstracts use "disease" most (220 names). Labels keep their own wording either way: "Wound healing disorder" stays, and "wound healing condition" merges into it. **I'd make "disease" the preferred word.**
- **Does "condition" always mean a disease?** "wound condition" (14 abstracts) reads as the state of the wound, not a disease. With the rule it would merge with "wound pathology". Should "condition" stay where it means a state, or merge everywhere as you said?

**3. Mixed-case abbreviations ("MSC-Exo", "i-PRF").** Rule 58 only catches abbreviations with no lowercase letters. A widened test (one token containing a run of two or more capitals) finds 328 more names (460 mentions). I ran your rule 58 on them dry, without writing:
- **70 have one full form in their own abstracts.** For example "i-PRF" → "injectable platelet-rich fibrin", "hs-CRP" → "high-sensitivity C-reactive protein", "DPP-4i" → "dipeptidyl peptidase-4 inhibitor". I still need to read these: "sTBI" came out as "traumatic brain injury" and lost the "severe".
- **7 are defined in several spellings that mean the same thing**, e.g. "NPWTi-d", "rhEGF", "WIfI". By your rule they're kept under one full form.
- **22 are defined, but the letters don't map cleanly onto the full form**, e.g. "HOCl" → "hypochlorous acid", "HIF-1alpha". They need reading.
- **238 are never defined → out of scope by your rule.** This includes "MSC-Exo". The test also catches things that aren't really abbreviations, and these would go out too:
  - brand names: "TeKBrace", "ESwab", "VarithenaTM";
  - chemistry: "MXene";
  - "microRNA", which is the full form you already map "miRNA" onto, so sending it out would clash.

  I'd keep "microRNA" and send the rest out.
- **Not covered at all: abbreviations inside longer names**, such as "PRP therapy", "VAS score", "MRSA infection". Rule 58 only covers names that are nothing but an abbreviation. Should it reach inside names too? That would expand them wherever one unambiguous full form is known.

**Effect on the review page.** All three rules rename and merge terms, so the synonym and superclass lists change. The two placement checks key on names; merged variants would drop out and keep the canonical name's result. New names that appear would need placing and checking again. I'd pause the [review page](https://claude.ai/artifact/QB3gqPiQvaJDGWhxoLD2aN), apply the naming rules, rerun placement for the changed names only, and then rebuild the page. The decisions you've made so far stay attached to the names that survive.

**Decisions for you:**
1. Is "disease" the preferred word?
2. Does "condition" stay where it means a state?
3. Should I keep "microRNA" and send the other undefined forms out?
4. Should rule 58 also expand abbreviations inside longer names?

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - **Project:** a wound ontology pipeline that builds a WONT addition on top of MILD/BFO, plus a thesis.
     - The ground truth WONT (`ground_truth_final.owl`) is never altered.
     - "Core" means `output/owl/wont_extended_core.ttl`, the WONT addition.
   - **Standing rules:**
     - No silent failure.
     - Back up before every write and read back after it.
     - Append design rules to INSTRUCTIONS.md.
     - Measure, don't estimate.
     - "No abbreviations ever", and "no guessing".
     - The ontology's label wins (rule 36).
     - The user's email is used only for identification.
   - **Pipeline order** (user: "we do fca first"): placements into the term dict → FCA → only then build the WONT addition. Do not jump to building `wont_extended_core.ttl`.
   - The user said writing placements "needs some prior work". This was never specified; ask before writing.
   - **Term "placement":** the user asked what it means. I explained it as superclass (subClassOf) or synonym (mapping). From now on, use the words "superclass" and "synonym".
   - **Where superclass/synonym results go:** into the term dict as pair judgements under a new source key, not a new dict.
     - Synonym = `same_class` plus a mapping (`to`).
     - Superclass = `narrower`.
     - Refused = a kept refusal.
     - `subsumption_candidates()` must also read the new source.
   - **The user accepts these via the review page:**
     - synonyms are reviewed one by one;
     - superclasses are accepted as a batch, with spot checks;
     - the user asked for an "accept all others" button, which was done.
   - **Naming decisions from the user:**
     1. "pressure injury" → "pressure ulcer" inside names (apply).
     2. "disorder wins" over illness/pathology/disease/dysfunction; "condition can stay where it is a condition".
     3. Widened abbreviations: "keep microRNA send the rest out".
     4. Abbreviations "inside names too please".
   - **One class per label** (user): "wont has its own disease class. dont reuse ogms. same with the protein class. we dont need several class of several ontologies with the same name that have some wont classes as children".
   - **Other fixes the user asked for:**
     - Refuse "wound healing → impaired wound healing" plus 11 more narrowing merges.
     - Apply the label restore (16 labels).
     - Add the 11 unplaceable terms to the Category Rulings UI. The user then ruled on them ("Im done with the categories").
   - **Most recent user question:** "where are we?". Answered with a status report; work then continued on checking the newly placed terms.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Term dict** (`TermMappingAndGate`, `data/cache/term_mapping_*.json`):
     - per-term records hold `to`, `pairs{canonical:{pass1,pass2,curator,refused}}`, `gate`, `category`, `senses` and `spelling` (alias);
     - `apply_renames` leaves aliases and supports reversal (`spelling_reversed`);
     - the DataManager prunes old cache versions; the backups in `data/backup` are the reliable copies.
   - **ConceptStore view:** the store is evidence; the view applies the term dict.
   - **Pass 0** (`Orchestrator.run_pass0_normalise`, `mappers/pass0.py`):
     - applies `canonical_vocabulary` inside names, keeps labels as written, and renames in the term dict;
     - `resolve_chains` is needed after renames;
     - `export_gate_lists` re-exports `config/blocklist.py` and `config/whitelist.py`.
   - **Canonical policy** (`TermMapper.enforce_canonical_policy`): rule 4 (new) says a label is never mapped away.
   - **Placement pipeline:**
     - `p4_place_terms.py` (sandbox `data/review/placement_2026-09-29.json`);
     - `placement_current_view.py` → `output/placement_current_2026-10-01.json`;
     - checks: `p4_verify_placements.py` (model yes/no), the verify-check chunks, and the self-check chunks;
     - `merge_placement_checks.py` → `data/review/placement_merged_2026-10-01.json`;
     - `elk_check_placements.py`.
   - **Reasoning:** ELK via ROBOT (`robot reason --reasoner ELK`); BFO disjointness lives in `output/owl/skeleton_tbox.ttl`.
   - **Review artifact** (db capability): one document per class group in the collections "synonym" and "subclass", fields `{kind,parent,iri,all,items{itemId:{v,term}}}`, item id = "i"+sha1(term)[:10].
   - **Abbreviation full forms:** read from the abstracts with Schwartz & Hearst (`expand_abbreviations.definitions`).

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`src/wound_ontology/mappers/term_mapper.py`**
     - Added rule 4 to `enforce_canonical_policy`: a label mapped to a non-label store spelling is turned round, unless the direction is curator-held (`method: curator` or `reverse` on the back pair).
     - The guard-refused case only unmaps the label.
     - Returns `rule4` keys: `labels_restored`, `labels_held`, `labels_unmapped`, `restored_entries`, `held_entries`, `unmapped_entries`, `moved_entries`.
   - **`src/wound_ontology/mappers/term_mapping_and_gate.py`**
     - `apply_renames` now also renames sense labels:
       ```python
       report["sense labels renamed"] = 0
       for rec in merged.values():
           senses = rec.get("senses")
           if not senses: continue
           for category, label in list(senses.items()):
               if look(label) != label:
                   senses[category] = look(label); report["sense labels renamed"] += 1
       ```
   - **`src/wound_ontology/utils/normalise.py`**
     - Added `PRESSURE_INJURY` and `_pressure_ulcer`.
     - Added `DISORDER_WORD`, `DISORDER_EPONYM_BEFORE` (possessive or eponym list), `DISORDER_KEEP`, `DISORDER_CONDITION` (a frozenset of about 90 disease-sense "condition" names), `CONDITION_WORD` and `_disorder`.
     - `DISORDER_KEEP` covers MODS, critical illness, tick-associated rash illness, illness perception/experience/belief/trajectory, pathology as a discipline, `pathology-guided/...`, plus proper names: Centers for Disease Control, burden of disease, classification of diseases, Diseases Society of America, infection diseases and travel medicine, digestive and kidney diseases, Ocular Surface Disease Index, peri-implant diseases and conditions.
     - Both rules are called in `canonical_vocabulary` after `_therapy_head`.
   - **Tests**
     - `tests/test_pass0.py`: new tests for pressure ulcer, disorder, kept words, a shared key, and proper names (checks "isease" in the output). An old example was changed to "infectious or inflammatory disorder".
     - `tests/test_spelling.py`: added `test_a_rename_reaches_the_sense_labels_of_a_two_senses_ruling`.
     - `tests/test_canonical_policy.py`: four rule-4 tests.
     - Full suite: 1,852 passed and 1 gate-list test failed because the lists were stale. After re-export, `tests/test_gate_lists_are_a_view.py` passes 12/12.
   - **`scripts/apply_label_restore.py`** (new): applies only rule 4. Written: 16 labels, 27 entries; held: "painful" and "threshold value".

2026-10-02 17:40:08 · Claude
I found a serious problem while checking the new names, and fixed it before going on: the earlier step that writes abbreviations out inside longer names had put wrong names into the term dict. Everything is now fixed, written to the term dict and read back. The new names are placed and checked, and the review page is rebuilt with your decisions carried over.

**What was wrong**

I read all 2,116 of those renames, and about 120 were wrong:
- **A full form taken from a single paper.** One abstract defined `DNA` as "deep neck abscess", so every name holding DNA followed it, e.g. `cell-free DNA` → "cell-free deep neck abscess". The same thing gave `alpha-SMA` → "alpha-supplementary motor area" and `SOD activity` → "semiocclusive dressing activity".
- **Truncated full forms.** `LLLT` became "level laser therapy" and `SSV` became "saphenous vein".
- **Smaller faults:** sentence fragments taken as full forms, doubled words ("wound bed score score"), abbreviations left inside the written-out form, and `IL` read as a Roman numeral, so `IL-6` was never written out.

**What I did**
- **Rewrote the step.** It now uses the name's own abstracts first. A full form must spell its abbreviation letter by letter, and when it is filled from other papers I read every row. That changed 441 names in the term dict and sent 56 out of scope. 330 of the 441 are names the first run had wrongly sent out of scope; they are back in with their correct full form.
- **Found a scope leak.** An abbreviation sent out of scope while still mapped to its full form took the full form out with it. For example, `DF` took "diabetic foot" out, and `%TBSA` took "total body surface area". There were 26 such cases. The abbreviation is now unmapped in each, so the full form keeps its own scope verdict.
- **Two more fixes.** "disease-modifying" and "coronavirus disease" are now protected from the disorder rule, and 8 names got the word "disease" back. "pathological disorder" (a MILD label) was out of scope by mistake; it is back in.
- **Placed and checked the renamed names.** 413 names were placed again for $0.07, and I read every new check row. ELK finds 0 unsatisfiable classes. All 1,863 tests pass.
- **Rebuilt the review page:** https://claude.ai/artifact/QB3gqPiQvaJDGWhxoLD2aN. Your decisions follow each term to its new name. A whole-group accept now covers only the terms you actually saw, so new terms show as undecided. 310 of the 319 synonyms carry your decision, and 3,690 superclass accepts and 3 refusals carried over. 164 decisions did not carry because the term went out of scope or is now under another class; these are listed by `scripts/carry_review_decisions.py`.

The rule changes are written up in INSTRUCTIONS.md (rule 62).

**Please confirm or overrule these judgements I made:**
1. **`DNA` → "deoxyribonucleic acid".** 7 of its 8 abstracts mean the molecule, and another abstract defines it that way (PMID 42579608). Your rule would otherwise have sent DNA, and every name containing it, out of scope.
2. **22 abbreviations sent out of scope** because their own abstracts use them for different things. Examples: `NLR` (neutrophil ratio, or negative likelihood ratio), `DF`, `PM`, `TM`, `CAD`, `EC`.
3. **Treated as one concept spelled differently:**
   - `MSC` = mesenchymal stem cell or stromal cell
   - `PBMT` = photobiomodulation (therapy)
   - plus three typos: `DFU`, `IMN`, `HIF-1alpha`
4. **`RNA` and `MRI` kept inside names**, like `microRNA`, because you had ruled them in scope as names.

**Two open questions:**
- **Scope from your own rulings.** 10 of your rulings from the contradiction queue put a synonym out of scope, and that also takes its full form out. Examples: "adjunctive therapy" takes "adjunct therapy" out, and "imaging" takes "imaging method" out. Is that what you meant?
- **Still waiting from before:** 9 terms have no scope verdict (e.g. "bone disorder", "pressure ulcer prevention program"). Shall I put them on the Category Rulings page?

**What was wrong:** the page loads earlier decisions from the store, and the store hands them back read-only. The first click on any group that already had decisions tried to edit that read-only copy and failed silently, so nothing changed and nothing saved. Your two earlier accepts worked only because those groups had no stored decisions yet. My carrying-over of your decisions put stored decisions on almost every synonym group, which is why it hit you everywhere now.

**The fix:** the page now edits a private copy of what it loads. If a click ever fails again, the footer says "That click failed: …" instead of doing nothing.

**Tested:** I made the test store return read-only data, like the real one. The old page then fails with exactly this error ("Cannot assign to read only property 'items'"). The new page accepts "refractory venous leg ulcer" and saves.

2026-10-02 19:22:54 · Claude
The page now has an **Exclude** button on every term, next to Accept and Refuse. It's republished at the same link; please reload.

- **What Exclude means:** the term itself isn't wanted, like "clinical method". It goes out of scope entirely and sits under no class. Refuse is different: it rejects only the proposed class, and the term stays in scope.
- **What you'll see:** an excluded term is greyed and struck through. Its group shows "N excluded", and the footer confirms each click, e.g. "Saved — excluded 'clinical method' under …".
- **Undo:** click Exclude again.
- **Finding them later:** the "Show" filter has a new "with exclusions" option that lists every group where you excluded something.
- **Tested:** I tested it against your stored decisions with read-only data, like the real store. Excluding saves, and nothing else in the group changes.

**One thing to watch:** "Accept all" or "Refuse all" on a group clears that group's per-term decisions, including exclusions. So exclude after any group-wide click, not before.

Nothing goes into the term dict until the step that writes your page decisions runs, and I haven't built that step yet. When I do, it will apply Exclude as an out-of-scope decision. I've added this to INSTRUCTIONS.md.

Yes, there is a subtree now. I wrote the placements into the term dict and rebuilt the class hierarchy, and you can browse it from any class downward:

**https://claude.ai/artifact/G3pD4zDmZrhRY5XP9PMUUA**

It opens on **Planned Process**, which has 146 direct children and 2,141 classes below it in total. Type any class name in the box to make it the root. Click ▸ to expand a branch, or click a name to jump into it. Each edge is tagged with where it came from:
- **curated**: written in WONT or MILD
- **placed**: one of your checked placements
- **text**: a single abstract said so

You can switch each kind off. I loaded it in a headless browser and it renders and expands.

**What I applied, following your "go on with what we have":**
- **Superclasses:** 13,143 kept. That is 3,809 you accepted plus 9,334 kept by the page's default, which was "counts as accepted unless refused". 3 you refused are recorded as refusals.
- **Synonyms:** all 318 you accepted are now mapped onto their class; the 1 you refused is recorded as a refusal.
- **Skipped:** 11 pairs were already refused in the term dict, so I left them out.
- **Read back from the term dict:** 13,122 placement edges and 317 review-mapped synonyms. The few fewer than above are the cases where one name had two rows; I didn't trace each one.
- **The hierarchy now holds** 13,119 placed edges, 540 curated and 32,677 from text.
- **Code and tests:** "placed" is a new source in the hierarchy, ranked below curated and above text. The script is `scripts/apply_placements.py`, the term dict was backed up first, and I added 8 tests, which pass.
- **The review page stays usable.** I wrote the decisions as they stood when I saved a copy of the page's store just before applying. Anything you mark after that (for example Exclude on "Clinical Method", which still sits under Planned Process) is not in yet. I'll pick those up on a later run.

**Two things you'll notice in the explorer:**
- **Some classes appear at the top level that shouldn't.** "Planned Process", "1-D Extent" and other classes from the imported modules show up without a parent. That's because the hierarchy only reads WONT and MILD's own subclass statements, not those of the imported modules. It's a gap in the view, not in the placements, and it needs fixing before the build.
- **Some junk names are still in scope.** Examples are "Cierre Lineal Primario" (Spanish) and "#NecrotisingSoftTissuesInfection". I'll handle these as problems come up, as you suggested, rather than reviewing the 10k terms by hand.

**Still running:** the reasoner check (ELK) on the rebuilt hierarchy. When it finishes, FCA runs next on the Wound subtree, now with the placements in it. Then I'll report what the reasoner and FCA turn up.

The tree still shows terms that come from a single abstract. Their parent edge is their basic type, such as process or material entity, taken from that abstract, not a placement. This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. **Primary Request and Intent**
   - **Project:** a wound ontology pipeline that builds a WONT addition on MILD/BFO, plus a thesis.
   - **Standing rules:**
     - No silent failure.
     - Back up before every write and read back after it.
     - Append design rules to INSTRUCTIONS.md.
     - Measure, don't estimate.
     - "No abbreviations ever", and "no guessing".
     - The ground truth WONT (`ground_truth_final.owl`) is never altered.
     - The user's email is used only for identification.
   - **Pipeline order** (user): placements → FCA → only then build the WONT addition.
   - **Latest direction from the user:** "If i go on lookig through the 10k terms, this will take ages. can you go on with what we have now and work through the rest with fca and proper ontology at the end that you can deal with the new problems popping up in the meantime".
   - **The user then chose:**
     - evidence cut "≥2 abstracts (Recommended)";
     - restatements "Yes, check and convert (Recommended)".
   - **Earlier in this stretch the user asked for:**
     - an Exclude button on the review page ("some are not necessary or nnspecific nonsense like clinical method");
     - fixes because clicks did nothing;
     - "is there a way to show me the subtree after planned process";
     - complained "Wound Analysis … Wound Scanning placed. so many nonsense terms. ouch".

2. **Key Technical Concepts**
   - **Term dict** (`TermMappingAndGate`):
     - pairs hold sources: pass1/pass2/curator/refused, plus the new `placement`;
     - gate verdicts (curator/pass2/pass1/rule) are pooled over a concept by `effective_gate_verdict`;
     - aliases come from `apply_renames`.
   - **Hierarchy sources and ranks:** `curated` 4 > `placed` 3 > `text` 2 > `fca` 1 (in `BaseHierarchy.get_data`).
   - **Evidence cut:** `PLACEMENT_MIN_ABSTRACTS = 2` in `orchestrator.py`.
   - **FCA:**
     - context matrix with conceptual scaling and attribute inheritance;
     - undecided (NaN) cells;
     - pessimistic and optimistic lattices;
     - the new `min_support` evidence cut on attributes.
   - **Review page:**
     - artifact db collections "synonym"/"subclass", one document per group;
     - item verdicts: accept, refuse, and the new exclude;
     - stored documents come back read-only (frozen).
   - **ELK** via ROBOT, used by `run_gate_hierarchy_edges`.

3. **Files and Code Sections**
   - **`scripts/expand_abbreviations_inside.py`** (version 2)
     - Sources in order: the name's own spelled definitions → curator SAME (KEEP lists) → own raw definitions (not when in `UNSPELLED_BAD`) → `WHOLE_REMAP` → `WHOLE_OUT` blocks → corpus definitions only if they agree on one concept (and known) → ontology synonym.
     - Processes token-first, right to left; recurses into the written-out form; de-duplicates words.
     - Excludes formulas, adding OH, CO, NO and NH; uses a fixed Roman-numeral regex.
     - `KEEP_PARTS` = microRNA plus abbreviations the curator kept as names (RNA, MRI).
     - Also revives first-run outs.
     - Report: `output/abbreviation_inside_v2_2026-10-02.json`.
   - **`scripts/expand_abbreviations.py`**
     - Added `spelled_definitions`, `_spelled_tail` and `dirty()`.
     - `spells(sf, lf, keep_stop=False)` now lets stop words count as letters.
   - **`scripts/fix_out_leaks.py`**
     - Unmaps (refuses the pair for) abbreviations put out by rule 58 whose out leaked onto the full form.
     - Also covers `WHOLE_OUT` terms swallowed by the full form's curator in.
   - **`scripts/restore_disease_proper_names.py`**: rerun after the new `DISORDER_KEEP` phrases.
   - **`src/wound_ontology/utils/normalise.py`**: `DISORDER_KEEP` now also matches `disease[\s-]+modifying` and `coronavirus[\s-]+disease`.
   - **`tests/test_pass0.py`**: 2 new parametrized cases.
   - **`scripts/placement_check_chunks.py`** (new): appends unread verify/self rows into new chunk files.
   - **Verdict files:** `data/review/placement_verifycheck_2026-10-01/verdicts_05.txt` and `verdicts_06.txt`; `placement_selfcheck_2026-10-01/verdicts_29.txt`–`verdicts_31.txt` (30 includes 16939:0).
   - **`scripts/carry_review_decisions.py`** (new): expands a group-level `all` into per-term decisions; never carries an `all`.
   - **`scripts/superclass_review_page.py`**
     - Edits a JSON copy of loaded documents; `onClick` is wrapped in try/catch.
     - Status message names each action; groups flash after a click.
     - Undecided groups first (`openAtStart` snapshot); undecided terms first inside a group.
     - Exclude button (`v: "exclude"`), "with exclusions" filter, pills.
     - Artifact: https://claude.ai/artifact/QB3gqPiQvaJDGWhxoLD2aN, version 7.
   - **`scripts/apply_placements.py`** (new)
     - Reads `placement_merged_2026-10-01.json` and decisions from `data/review/superclass_review_decisions_2026-10-02_final/`.
     - Superclass → `placement: {verdict: "narrower", iri, check, by, when}`.
     - Accepted synonym → `to` plus curator `same_class`.
     - Refuse → `gt.refuse`; exclude → curator gate out.
     - Skips pairs already refused.
   - **`src/wound_ontology/mappers/term_mapping_and_gate.py`**
     - New `placements()`: returns (term, canonical) pairs with `placement.verdict == "narrower"`; skips refused pairs, aliases, mapped terms and out-of-scope terms.
   - **`src/wound_ontology/hierarchies/base_hierarchy.py`**
     - New `SOURCE_PLACED = "placed"`; `SOURCE_RANK` = {curated: 4, placed: 3, text: 2, fca: 1}.
     - `get_data(..., placed=())` stamps those edges `placed`.
   - **`src/wound_ontology/hierarchies/curated_seed.py`**
     - New `module_ancestry(iris, module_sources)`: walks `rdfs:subClassOf` upward in the import modules, from the given classes only.
   - **`src/wound_ontology/orchestrator.py`**
     - `PLACEMENT_MIN_ABSTRACTS = 2`.
     - `run_build_hierarchies` reads `placements()`, applies the evidence cut (abstract counts from `store.view()`), adds module ancestry above the placement parent IRIs to `predefined_df`, and passes `placed=` on.
     - `run_fca(..., min_support=1)` passes `min_support` through.
   - **`src/wound_ontology/fca/context_matrix.py`**
     - `get_data(..., min_support=1)` counts support per (entity, triple) across abstracts and drops measured attributes below the cut before scaling.
   - **Tests:** `test_hierarchy_provenance.py` gained 3 placed-source tests; `tests/test_placements_view.py` is new with 5 tests. Both pass.
   - **`scripts/p4_restatement_check.py`** (new): model answers same/narrower/unclear → `data/review/placement_restatement_2026-10-02.json`.
   - **`scripts/apply_restatements.py`** (new): reads Claude's reading file `data/review/placement_restatement_read_2026-10-02.txt` (45 kept ids) and maps the other 79 `same` rows.
   - **`scripts/hierarchy_explorer_page.py`** (new)
     - Tree page from the latest `class_hierarchy_provenance_*.csv`.
     - Artifact: https://claude.ai/artifact/G3pD4zDmZrhRY5XP9PMUUA, version 2.

     - Rule 62 extended (second incident, scope leak).

   - **Memory:** `project_placement_checks.md` updated.

2026-10-02 21:15:23 · Claude
I added both inheritance modes and ran them on the Wound subtree. The data picks **class-level inheritance**, so that is now the default. It barely matters, though: the real limit is that only 29 of the 322 Wound classes have any property stated in two or more abstracts.

**What changed**
- **Unstated means false, for FCA only.** A property no abstract states of a class now counts as not holding. That gives one lattice in which every concept is certain. The ontology itself stays open-world.
- **"Class level" is decided by a test, not a cut-off.** A parent's property passes down only if its own subclasses state it significantly more often than everything outside that branch does. The test is a one-sided Fisher exact test with a 5% false-discovery rate (Benjamini–Hochberg).
  - A guessed share of abstracts couldn't work: the strongest property of Wound, "wound area", appears in only 3.2% of its 1,013 abstracts.
  - On Wound, 48 of the 129 parent–property pairs tested pass.

**The comparison** (322 classes × 94 properties; no naming calls). I added "none" as a third, baseline mode.

| Inheritance | Inherited cells | Hierarchy edges contradicted | New groupings | Share of grouping cells members state themselves |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| everything | 20,564 | 0 / 326 | 5 | 1% |
| class-level | 11,231 | 174 / 326 | 5 | 22% |
| none | 0 | 197 / 326 | 18 | 100% |

- **Everything vs class-level:** both propose the same 5 groupings. Under "everything", each grouping also pulls in 20–52 subclasses that state none of the shared properties.
- **Contradicted edges:** class-level contradicts 174 of 326 hierarchy edges, but this costs nothing downstream. FCA is the lowest-ranked source and nothing from it enters the hierarchy without review.
- **None:** it gives 18 groupings, but 9 of them rest on misread subclass statements (see below).

**What FCA found**
- **One real signal:** Diabetic Foot Ulcer, Pressure Ulcer and Ulcus Cruris Mixtum share chronic-wound properties (pain, wound area, healing time, depth), yet none of them sits under Chronic Wound. In the ground-truth WONT, Diabetic Foot Ulcer hangs only under Diabetic Wound.
- **A new problem:** statements like "Heel Pressure Ulcer part_of Pressure ulcer" or "Wound part_of Wound" are subclass statements misread as part-of. They even pass the class-level test. They must not become part-of axioms when I build the WONT addition, and I've recorded that.

**Tests and records**
- 9 new tests cover the closed world, the class-level test, untestable parents and the abstract cut; all FCA tests pass.
- The full unit suite at first showed 8 failures. They came from this afternoon's placement wiring, not from FCA: the test stand-ins didn't know the new `placed` source. I updated them and that file now passes (10 of 10). I didn't rerun the whole suite afterwards; the other 1,825 tests had already passed.
- Rule 65 is in INSTRUCTIONS.md with the table above; the file was backed up first and read back after.
- Full comparison, every test result and all groupings: `data/review/fca_inheritance_comparison_2026-10-02.json`.

**Next:** build the WONT addition, applying the ≥2-abstract cut to text edges too and keeping out the misread part-of statements. Then HermiT, BioPortal and the competency questions. I'll also put the 5 FCA groupings on the review page; the Chronic Wound question is the only one with substance.

2026-10-02 22:33:18 · Claude
Opus 5.5 is good at filling the gaps, but on the Chronic Wound subtree it mainly shows that the hierarchy there is flat, not that new superclasses are missing. And the WONT addition exists, but only as a version from before today's changes.

**The trial**
- **Subtree:** Chronic Wound, 32 classes × 94 properties = 3,008 cells. The model judged every cell as `all` (true of essentially every instance), `some`, `no` or `unsure`. It also judged the cells the abstracts already state, so its agreement with them could be measured.
- **Nothing was written into the store.** The verdicts are in `data/review/fca_fill_trial_chronic_wound_2026-10-02.json`; the script is `scripts/fca_fill_trial.py`.

| Context | True cells | Concepts | New groupings |
|---|---|---|---|
| Abstracts only (≥2 abstracts) | 18 | 10 | 0 |
| Abstracts + class-level inheritance (current) | 1,126 | 5 | 0 |
| Abstracts + model `all` | 1,205 | 83 | 53 |

- **Model answers:** 1,197 `all`, 1,102 `some`, 709 `no`, 0 `unsure`.
- **On cells nobody states:** 27% `all`. On cells that inheritance adds: 61% `all`.
- **Where it disagrees with the abstracts, it is right.** Of the 18 stated cells it confirms 10. It calls 4 `some`: pain, inflamed, hypoxia and lower-extremity location, which are indeed not true of every chronic wound. It calls 4 `no`, and those are exactly the junk: three misread "part_of: wound" statements and "material disease entity" as a quality.
- **The 53 groupings come from very few properties.** 30 properties are true of everything and 42 of nothing. Almost all of the 53 are combinations of four splits:
  - ulcer vs. other wound (17 classes),
  - lower-extremity location (10),
  - non-healing,
  - exudate type.

**What that shows:** all 31 classes hang directly under Chronic Wound. The groupings mostly say how existing classes should nest; for example, Chronic Leg/Foot/Heel Ulcer under Chronic Ulcer and under Chronic Lower-Extremity Wound. That is more useful than invented superclasses, but each proposal still needs review.

**Cost and credit**
- The exact cost isn't shown yet because OpenRouter's usage figure lags. From the token counts I estimate about $0.60 for the 32 classes.
- **The OpenRouter account is nearly empty.** It has about $0.75 at most, now probably less. Requests failed with "402 in-flight budget exhausted" until I retried them one at a time.
- The whole Wound subtree (322 classes) would cost roughly $6. That needs a top-up first.

**The WONT addition**
- `output/owl/wont_extended_core.ttl` exists. It was built on 1 October at 00:59 and has 966 classes: 354 placed terms plus candidates.
- On 1 October you set the order: placements into the term dict, then FCA, then the build. That file was made before that order was followed. It contains none of today's work:
  - the renaming rules (2,116 names renamed, 1,026 put out),
  - the 13,146 checked placements, of which 3,107 pass the ≥2-abstract cut,
  - the 79 restatements turned into synonyms,
  - the exclusions from the review page.
- "Building" it means regenerating it from the current state, not starting from scratch. HermiT, BioPortal and the competency questions would then run on the new file.

**Decision for you:** if the gap-filling looks worth it, top up OpenRouter and I'll run the full Wound subtree, then put the nesting proposals on the review page before the rebuild.

2026-10-03 02:23:53 · Claude
The matrix with all 3,008 verdicts is on a page: https://claude.ai/artifact/DkTkLZ5AKEe6mMUkHeUyQS

**What it shows**
- **One row per class, one column per property:** 32 classes and 94 properties. Each cell is the model's answer: `all` (true of essentially every instance), `some` or `no`.
- **Markers:** an amber ring marks the 18 cells stated in two or more abstracts. A dot marks the 1,108 cells that class-level inheritance adds.
- **Detail:** pointing at or tapping a cell shows the class, the property, the verdict and how many abstracts state it.
- **Column views:**
  - the 22 properties that split the classes (the default),
  - the 30 the model calls true of every class,
  - the 42 it never calls true of every class,
  - all 94.
- **Rows** are grouped by answer pattern by default, so blocks are visible. The number after each class name is how many abstracts name it.
- **Below the matrix:** the 18 cells the abstracts state, with the model's answer to each, and the counts for the 22 splitting properties.

**What to look at**
- **The 4 times the model says `no` to something the abstracts state:**
  - "Chronic Wound part of wound" (7 abstracts),
  - "Hard-To-Heal Wound part of wound" (5),
  - "Lower-Extremity Hard-To-Heal Wound part of hard-to-heal wound" (2),
  - "Chronic Wound has quality material disease entity" (2).

  The first three are subclass statements misread as part-of; the fourth is a category recorded as a quality.
- **The three blocks in the splitting view:**
  - the ulcer classes (ulcer area, size, severity, volume: 17 classes),
  - the lower-extremity ones (10),
  - the non-healing ones (26).

  These are what produce the 53 groupings, and they suggest how the 31 flat classes should nest.

2026-10-03 02:32:25 · Claude
Agreed. Of the 94 columns, 63 can never separate one class from another, and dropping them leaves a small, sensible result.

**What was wrong with the 94 columns.** I read every one; the reading is saved in `data/review/fca_column_reading_2026-10-02.json`.

| Kind | Count | Examples | Why it's useless for grouping |
|---|---|---|---|
| A measurable dimension, not a value | 42 | wound area, depth, time to healing, pH level, ulcer size, exudate type | Every wound has an area. "Has a size" is true of all classes, so it separates nothing. |
| A misread subclass statement | 11 | part of: wound, part of: pressure ulcer | "Heel pressure ulcer part of pressure ulcer" is really "is a". |
| One wound's state at one time | 5 | complete healing, healed state, epithelialized | Describes one patient's wound at follow-up, not a kind of wound. |
| Category error | 5 | quality: material disease entity, part of: diabetes mellitus, risk factor | A class or category recorded as a property. |
| **Real values** | **31 → 26** | malodorous, painful, infected, macerated, neuropathic, lower extremity, amputation risk | Spelling variants merged: malodor / malodour / malodorous, and pain / painful condition / wound pain. |

**FCA on the 26 clean columns, reusing Opus's existing verdicts (no new cost):**

| Context | True cells | Concepts | Groupings |
|---|---|---|---|
| Abstracts only | 7 | 4 | 0 |
| Abstracts + model `all` | 183 | 16 | 6 |

The 53 groupings shrink to 6, and they all say one of three things:
- Chronic leg, foot and heel ulcers belong under **Chronic Lower Extremity Wound** (11 classes; 9 of them are also non-healing).
- **Chronic Heel Ulcer** goes with **Chronic Foot Ulcer** (foot location).
- **Chronic Foot Ulcer** and **Chronic Neuropathic Ulcer** share amputation risk.

The earlier "ulcer vs. wound" split is gone. It only existed because of columns like "ulcer area" and "ulcer size"; whether something is an ulcer is in the class name, not in any property.

**Why it happens:** extraction records measurable dimensions and actual values the same way, as `has_quality`. Attribute values also never pass through the term dict, so spelling variants stay separate columns.

**This also matters for the WONT addition.** If store attributes become axioms, "Chronic Wound has quality some wound area" and "Chronic Wound part of some Wound" would end up in the ontology. I haven't yet checked how the export turns attributes into axioms; I'll do that before the rebuild.

**Proposal:**
1. FCA uses only columns read as real values, with variants merged, through the reading file above.
2. I read the attribute values for the rest of the store the same way, in batches. The model can pre-sort them; I check every row.
3. When OpenRouter has credit again, the gap-filling runs on the clean columns only: 26 columns instead of 94 cuts the cost by about two thirds.

Do you want me to apply this as the rule and wire it into the FCA code?

2026-10-03 02:42:07 · Werkzeug Bash: Record rule 66 and the precision preference
2026-10-03 02:42:24 · Claude
Yes, they often differ. A relation's value frequently has a different category as a term than the relation requires. Across the store, 2,097 of 26,372 relations break the category rule at the subject or the value, and 3,214 more can't be decided.

**The check** (`scripts/relation_typing_check.py`): each relation is tested at both ends, the term's own category against what the predicate requires (has quality needs a quality, has disposition a disposition, part of the same kind on both sides, and so on).

| | Relations | Category right on both ends | Wrong | Can't be decided |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| All | 26,372 | 21,061 | 2,097 | 3,214 |
| Stated in ≥2 abstracts | 1,349 | 1,007 | 107 | 235 |

Your examples:
- "Wound has quality **contamination**" fails, because contamination is typed as a material entity (the contaminant).
- "Wound has quality **contaminated**" passes; it is typed as a quality.
- So the two are not synonyms. The typing settles which reading is meant, and only the quality one is a sound attribute.
- "Diabetic foot ulcer has quality material disease entity" fails as well (6 abstracts). So do "time to healing" (a time span) and "complete wound closure" (an end point of a process).

**Why ELK let it through:** the reasoner's base ontology carries no domain or range for has quality, inheres in or has disposition, so a material thing as a quality contradicts nothing. Adding the standard domains and ranges from BFO/RO would make ELK enforce the same check. I'd test first that WONT itself stays consistent with them. The relation gate also last ran on 1 October, before today's renames.

**Merging:** a separate mapper for attributes isn't needed. The store view already passes attribute values through the term dict, so a mapping applies there automatically. Repairing wrong values doesn't pay either: of 156 wrongly typed values, only 4 have a same-stem twin of the right category. Wrong values get dropped. What's still missing is merging true synonyms of the same category, such as malodor / malodour / malodorous, which are all qualities. I haven't yet checked why the term dict never merged those.

**On the models' ontological thinking:** in the trial, Opus said "some" for contaminated on 31 of the 32 Chronic Wound classes, and "all" only for Colonized Chronic Wound, so it did not overstate. The overstatement comes from two places in our pipeline:
- The export writes every relation as "every C has some T". 23,392 of its 24,621 relations rest on one abstract.
- My class-level test from rule 65 passed "contamination" for Wound. A property that is common among a class's subclasses is not necessarily true of every instance. That was a mistake in my test.

**What a small, sound version looks like:**
1. ≥2 abstracts: 26,372 → 1,349 relations.
2. Category right on both ends: 1,007.
3. A true-of-every-instance reading (all / some): only "all" becomes an axiom. "Some" (contaminated wounds) could at most become a defined subclass like "Contaminated wound", never a property of Wound.
4. Part-of between a wound and a wound class (a misread subclass statement) is dropped.

Only what survives step 3 goes into the WONT addition, and FCA uses only those values.

I've recorded this as rule 66, along with two hierarchy faults found on the way: Role sits under Quality, and Disposition and Function have no parent. Your preference is also saved in memory.

**Decision for you:** I can read the 1,007 relations for "all vs some" myself now, which needs no OpenRouter credit. The alternative is to wait for credit so Opus pre-sorts them before I read. Which do you want?

2026-10-03 03:06:15 · Claude
I read all 1,007 relations myself. **203 hold for every instance and can become axioms.** 537 hold only for some instances, and 267 are wrong.

**From store to axioms**

| Step | Relations |
|---|---|
| All relations in the store | 26,372 |
| Stated in ≥2 abstracts | 1,349 |
| …and category right on both ends | 1,007 |
| …and true of every instance (my reading) | **203** |

I read each row as the axiom it would become: "every C has R to some T". If I was unsure between "all" and "some", I chose "some". The criteria are in `data/review/relation_universality_2026-10-02/README.txt`. The verdicts and a short reason for every "no" are in `verdicts_01.txt` to `verdicts_10.txt`. The compile script checked that every row has exactly one verdict.

**What survives (203), for example:**
- **Properties of a whole class:** Burn injury has some depth and some size; Diabetic foot ulcer is located on the foot; scars have height, pliability and vascularity.
- **Participants by definition:** debridement involves a wound and devitalized tissue; dressing change involves a dressing; NPWT involves a wound, a dressing and an NPWT system.
- **What instruments are about:** the Bates-Jensen tool is about wounds, the Vancouver and POSAS scar scales are about scars, Wagner grade is about diabetic foot ulcers, FLACC is about pain.
- **Parts:** platelet-rich plasma contains growth factors; a honey-based dressing contains honey; re-epithelialization is part of wound healing.

**Reported only for some instances (537), not axioms:**
- "Wound has quality contaminated", since there are clean wounds.
- 141 of the 150 "takes part in healing / treatment" relations, because not every wound heals or is treated.
- All 42 causal links ("healing follows NPWT").

**Wrong (267):**
- 180 are subclass statements misread as part-of: "Acute wound part of Wound", "amputation part of surgical procedure", "antibiotic has part meropenem".
- 26 relate a class to itself.
- 7 put a property on the wrong bearer: "wound pain inheres in the wound" (the pain is the patient's), and "total body surface area" given as a quality of the burn (it belongs to the body).

**Consequences**
- **The export has to change.** It currently writes all 24,621 store relations as axioms. It should write only these 203.
- **FCA has almost nothing left to work with.** Most surviving properties hold for every wound (area, depth, size), so they separate nothing. FCA's useful output stays what we saw earlier: nesting proposals among existing classes, not new superclasses.
- Rule 66 in INSTRUCTIONS.md and memory are updated with these numbers.

**Next, unless you object:**
1. Switch the export to the 203.

3. Fix Role sitting under Quality.

**1. Export: only the 203 relations read as true of every instance**
- The export no longer writes all 26,372 store relations as axioms, and no longer writes the 4,724 unread part-of edges either.
- 121 of the 203 land on new classes. The other 82 sit on existing WONT classes and are withheld, because WONT is never changed.
- Passing the whole store together with the read relations is now refused.

**2. Domains and ranges: in the reasoner's base, and they work**
- The base ontology now takes BFO's 35 subclass edges, 39 disjointness pairs, and 15 domains and 14 ranges for our relations from the import modules (RO/BFO/IAO/OBI).
- ELK now rejects "X has quality some material disease entity", "X has quality some process" and "X participates in some material entity", and accepts "has quality some quality".
- Two domains are written as unions, which ELK can't handle; they are left out and reported.

**3. Why Role sat under Quality, and the fix**
- **Cause:** our hierarchy merges classes by label. The OBI module says ChEBI's "role" ⊑ PATO's "quality", which is deliberate there, so that ChEBI's classes have a parent. Yesterday's import-ancestry step climbed into ChEBI "role" and filed its parent as the parent of BFO's Role. BFO's own axioms were never read, and MILD's modules held only 20 of BFO's 36 classes.
- **Fixed:**
  - The module build now pulls in all of BFO: 36/36 classes, 35/35 edges. No other module changed.
  - The hierarchy now has BFO's complete tree. Role ⊑ Realizable Entity; Disposition, Function and Process Boundary have their correct parents.
  - A non-BFO class with a BFO label (ChEBI role, PATO quality) can no longer give a BFO node its parent.
  - All 20 BFO classes that are not typing categories (object, site, fiat object part, history, relational quality…) are now grounding targets in the extraction schema. The 16 typing categories themselves are unchanged.

**4. Rebuild**
- **Core:** 3,095 new classes, all hung by checked placements, with 2,070 BFO typings and 55 read relations. 4,204 text-only classes go to `wont_candidates.ttl`. The old head-noun placer is replaced by `scripts/build_wont_core.py`.
- **ELK (core plus import closure):** 11 unsatisfiable. 4 are WONT's own (explained below); the other 7 are new classes placed under them (MRSA, MSSA, MDR S. aureus, S. aureus isolate, ESBL E. coli, Acinetobacter baumannii, burn-wound pathogen).
- **HermiT:** still running. The baseline on WONT alone has taken 73 minutes so far, the core 23. The ChEBI/DRON-heavy closure is slow for HermiT.

**Found on the way: the hierarchy's reasoner check had been reporting a broken result as "clean"**
- It ignored ROBOT's exit code, so "ontology is inconsistent" counted as passed. That covers the "ELK clean" results I reported yesterday and today.
- The inconsistency came from WONT's 48 named individuals (Wagner grades, burn zones) sitting under classes that text edges had emptied.
- It also blamed only the emptied class's own edges. "Nutrient ⊑ Material Entity" (from the text) left nothing that could be protein's nutrient role, so it dropped 31 checked placements under Protein instead.
- **Fixed:** a failed run now raises an error; individuals are left out of the check; and before a placed or curated edge is dropped, the reasoner is asked for an explanation.
- **Result:** 1,127 edges removed, all from text. Every curated (882) and placed (3,107) edge kept; the hierarchy has 0 unsatisfiable classes.

All 1,842 tests pass, 11 of them new. Backups are in `data/backup/`, and rules 66 and 67 in INSTRUCTIONS.md record all of this.

**Decision needed:** WONT/MILD give Staphylococcus aureus, E. coli and "pathogen with wound infecting tendency" a *tendency* through **has disposition**. MILD itself declares disposition, role and tendency mutually exclusive, and RO says has disposition points to a disposition. So under its own imports, WONT has 4 unsatisfiable classes (those three plus MRSA infection), and that blocks a clean reasoner run. Until you rule, I've left that one range out of the reasoner's base ontology. I recommend giving tendencies their own relation (e.g. "has tendency") in MILD and using it in those 3 axioms; the alternative is to drop the disjointness between tendency and disposition. Which do you want?

**1. Role sat under Quality, and BFO was incomplete**
- **Cause:** the hierarchy matches classes by label. The OBI module files ChEBI's "role" under PATO's "quality", so ChEBI role's parent became BFO Role's parent. BFO's own subclass axioms were never read, and MILD's modules held only 20 of BFO's 36 classes.
- **Modules:** the module build script now pulls in all of BFO, and the modules are rebuilt: 36/36 classes, 35/35 edges.
- **Hierarchy:** it now has BFO's complete tree, and every BFO class has exactly its BFO parent: Role ⊑ Realizable Entity, Disposition ⊑ Realizable Entity, Function ⊑ Disposition. Information entity sits under generically dependent continuant, tendency under realizable entity.
- A non-BFO class with a BFO label (ChEBI role, PATO quality) can no longer give a BFO class its parent.
- **Extraction schema:** all 20 BFO classes that are not typing categories (object, site, fiat object part, history, relational quality…) are now offered there.

**2. Reasoner check: domains, ranges and disjointness**
- The base ontology for the ELK checks now takes BFO's subclass edges, its 39 disjointness pairs, and the domains and ranges of our relations from RO/BFO/IAO/OBI.
- ELK now rejects "has quality some material disease entity", "has quality some process" and "participates in some material entity".

- Subclass edges: curated and placed in full; text edges only for terms named in ≥2 abstracts.
- **New core builder** (replaces the head-noun placer): a new class enters only through a checked placement, with its BFO typing. ELK then settles the rest: a class it rejects loses its relations, then moves to the candidates.
- **Result:** 3,095 new classes in the core, with 55 relations. 4,204 text-only classes go to `wont_candidates.ttl`. 82 read relations sit on existing WONT classes and stay out, because WONT isn't changed.

**4. The hierarchy's reasoner check had been reporting failures as "clean"**
- **Silent failure:** it ignored ROBOT's exit code, so "ontology is inconsistent" counted as passed. Earlier "ELK clean" results, including yesterday's, were not real.
- **Why it was inconsistent:** text edges emptied classes that hold WONT's 48 named individuals (Wagner grades, burn zones…).
- **Blame:** it only blamed the emptied class's own edges. "Nutrient ⊑ Material Entity" from the text made protein's nutrient role impossible, so the check dropped 31 checked placements under Protein instead of that one text edge.
- **Fixed:** a failed run now raises an error; individuals are left out of the check; and before a checked edge is dropped, the reasoner is asked for an explanation.
- **Result:** 1,127 edges removed, all from text. Every curated (882) and placed (3,107) edge kept; the hierarchy has 0 unsatisfiable classes.

- **Rule:** a pair shifts when its value is most often typed tendency across the whole store, and the abstracts stating the pair never type it disposition or function.

- **Why not the simpler rule:** my first draft followed only the asserting abstract's typing. It would have made Osteomyelitis, Smoking and diabetic peripheral neuropathy into tendencies, so I tightened it.

- **The attributes were mostly junk.** Of 94 columns, 42 were measurement dimensions true of every wound (area, depth), 11 were misread subclass statements ("part of: pressure ulcer"), 5 were one-time states (healed), and 5 were category errors. That started the store-wide checks: the both-ends typing check (rule 66), the "true of every instance" reading (203 of 26,372 relations survived), and today's strict export. That is FCA's real, indirect contribution: it exposed that the relation layer was unfit to become axioms.
- **The model fill showed flatness, not missing superclasses.** With Opus filling gaps on clean columns, Chronic Wound yielded 6 groupings, all saying existing classes should nest. The leg, foot and heel ulcers belong under Chronic Lower Extremity Wound, and DFU, pressure ulcer and ulcus cruris mixtum share chronic-wound qualities without sitting under Chronic Wound. These were never put on a review page and are not in the ontology.

**Why it can't do more now**
- With precision first, its input is the 203 read relations. Most of those are qualities every wound has, which you've just placed on Wound once, so they separate nothing.
- A useful run would need gap-filled values from a strong model across all subtrees. That needs OpenRouter credit (about $6 for the Wound subtree) plus a reading of every proposal.

**My recommendation**
- **For the build:** take the nesting proposals from the trial as a small review list, which costs nothing because the verdicts exist. Don't run FCA as a source of new superclasses.
- **For the thesis:** report FCA as tested and why it didn't add structure: sparse, instance-level, category-mixed attributes. That negative result is what motivated the typing and universality checks.

Do you want the nesting proposals on a review page, or should FCA stay out of the build?

2026-10-04 20:40:43 · Autor
Fix the unstable numbering
2026-10-04 20:41:05 · Claude
Plan: a **persistent id registry**, a file that maps each new class (by its normalised name) to its WONT id.
- The export reuses a registered id instead of counting afresh.
- A brand-new class gets the next number after everything ever issued, so an id is never reused.
- A renamed class keeps its id, because the old spelling is looked up through the term dict.
- When two registered classes merge, one id survives and the other is recorded as obsolete, *replaced by* the survivor. That is standard OBO practice, so a published id never just disappears.
- Today's numbering becomes the seed.

2026-10-04 20:50:50 · Claude
The numbering is fixed: two exports in a row now give every one of the 7,212 classes the pipeline creates the same id and the same name. The rebuilt core has 2,953 new classes and 0 unsatisfiable classes under ELK.

**How it works now**
- **A registry file** (`data/registry/wont_ids.json`) holds a permanent id for every class the pipeline creates. Spelling variants (case, plural, hyphen, ulcer/wound…) count as the same class. The export asks it before numbering anything.
- **Same class next build → same id.** A brand-new class gets the next number after every id ever issued, so no id is reused.
- **Renamed class → same id.** It's found through its former spellings in the term dict. A former spelling only counts once it no longer exists as a class of its own; otherwise two different classes would have been merged.
- **Two classes merged by a rename → one id survives,** and the other is kept as obsolete, "replaced by" the survivor. That's standard OBO practice: no id silently disappears.
- **Own number range:** pipeline ids start at **WONT_0500000**, so they can never collide with classes you add to WONT by hand (your main series stays below 100000; MILD's block is 1200xx). If a registry id ever shows up as a WONT class, the export stops.
- The file is backed up before every write and checked after it.

**Two problems the first test run caught, both fixed before seeding for good:**
- A former spelling that was still its own class got merged with its renamed class ("endovascular therapy"). Only spellings no longer in use count now.
- Stored names flipped between capitalisation variants ("Burn Injury" / "burn injury") from run to run. A name now only changes on a real rename.

Because the ids changed once with the move to the new range, I stopped HermiT and restarted it on this core. I'll report when it finishes.

All 1,868 tests pass, 7 of them new for the registry. Rule 71 in INSTRUCTIONS.md and memory are updated; memory notes not to delete or hand-edit the registry file.

**Still open:** once ids are published, an obsolete id should appear in the release as a deprecated class pointing to its replacement. Nothing is published yet, so for now that's only kept in the registry.
2026-10-04 23:38:45 · Autor
I don’t get it. Why can’t wont have consecutive ids?
2026-10-04 23:39:02 · Claude
They can be consecutive. Within the new range they are: WONT_0500000, 0500001, 0500002, … with no gaps. What I avoided is continuing straight after WONT's own last id (WONT_0000474), and the reason is collisions, not the numbering itself.

**The collision:** WONT's own file only knows its own classes. If the pipeline takes 475 onwards and you later add a class to WONT by hand (in Protégé, say), you'd naturally give it 475, the next free number in WONT. But 475 already belongs to a pipeline class in the addition. Two different classes would then share one IRI, and loading both files would quietly merge them into one class. The separate range makes that impossible however both sides grow.

**Gaps will appear over time either way.** When a class is dropped or merged, its id is retired, never reused, so the published ones stay unambiguous.

**If you'd rather have WONT_0000475 onwards, that works too, with one condition:** new WONT classes must never be numbered independently of the registry. That holds if either:
- you stop adding classes to WONT by hand, and the addition becomes the way WONT grows; or
- you set Protégé's automatic IDs to start above the registry's highest number.

Nothing is published yet, so switching costs nothing now: one re-seed, about a minute.

Do you want to keep the 0500000 range, or switch to continuing from 0000475?
2026-10-04 23:43:17 · Autor

2026-10-04 23:43:26 · Claude
Then the pipeline continues WONT's own series. The registry starts right after WONT's highest id (WONT_0000475) and stays consecutive. To make your Protégé reset easy, every export will print the next free WONT id and write it to a small file. The safety net stays: if a hand-made WONT class ever takes an id the registry already holds, the export stops and names it, instead of merging two classes.
